Streams explicitly choose and advertise re-admission per emitted page or a bounded authorized lease, with revocation and expiry behavior tested. Root-issuance daily ceilings retain the book's calendar-day and time-zone policy under TimeEvidence, with the charged day epoch recorded. These rules are executable security registry rows and acceptance vectors before the first expiry-gated surface ships.

### 14.3 Segregation of duties

`sod_policy.toml` is data: for each dangerous class (`Restatement`, `PriorPeriodAdjustment`, `PeriodControl::Reopen|Lock`, `PolicyControl::ActivateRule`, `ErasureCut`, `IssueCapability(ops ∋ restate)`), the number of approvers, the roles they must hold, and the exclusions (an approver cannot have prepared any entry in the case; the two approvers of a restatement cannot share a session lineage; an agent cannot approve its own draft's promotion). The kernel evaluates the policy at commit (FA-INV-10 clause `sod`), so a violation is a refusal with the exact rule cited, never a report finding after the fact — though `jet.self_approved` runs anyway, because a capability misconfiguration should be found twice.

### 14.4 Profiles

Registered capability templates: **Agent** (branch-confined, `requires_dry_run` on every write, amount ceiling, account scope, no `approve`/`close`/`restate`; creation of `Party`/`Instrument` and bank-identifier changes on a `Party` are schema-control effects on a draft promoted only by a human `Principal` — the fictitious-vendor and business-email-compromise vectors; above a registered ceiling, approvers of agent-prepared work must be `Principal.kind = Human`, and an agent approver never shares a `program`/`model` family with the preparer); **Preparer**; **Approver**; **Controller** (close, reopen with case, rule change with dual control); **Auditor** (read, verify, produce bundles, witness; the full known-at range by default — the population is frozen by immutability and by the witnessed checkpoint the firm takes at fieldwork start, not by blinding the auditor to the subsequent events it must evaluate through the report date under AS 2801 / AU-C 560; a `known_at ≤ seq` caveat remains available for examination-scope limits; redaction contract for privileged docs); **Regulator/Examiner** (as Auditor, with the examination's scope and expiry, full access logging); **Investigator** (as Regulator plus `hold`); **Receiver/Trustee** (Controller plus `hold` on a receivership branch, no `erase`; §15.6); **Production** (a one-shot capability that can only produce one named bundle under one redaction contract); **Witness** (sign checkpoints only). Every external profile's reads are fully logged (§9.7).

### 14.5 Access audit and non-repudiation

Every capability use is attributable to a principal and, for agents, to a program, model, and session; approvals and attestations bind the digest of what was approved; the access log is append-only and queryable; the transparency log binds the whole history to witnessed roots. A principal cannot later deny an approval it signed, and the book cannot later deny what a witness signed.

### 14.6 Erasure versus retention

§8.5's law, enforced here: erasure is a typed control entry under the `erase` capability with dual control; a legal hold blocks it; the audit log records it; amounts and lineage are never in scope.

---

## 15. Teller: The Agent and Human Surface (Bet A6, part 2)

### 15.1 Design stance

AI agents are the primary users of this system; humans are the primary *authorities*. The surface is designed under one rule: **the first thing an agent instinctively tries must work, or be redirected with a precise, copy-pasteable fix** — and nothing an agent can type by accident may damage the books. The agent-ergonomics Polish Bar is adopted verbatim as a release gate (§19, G3): first-try success on bare invocation and `--help`; `--json`/robot mode on every read; `capabilities --json` and `robot-docs guide` in-tool; at least one mega-command; a documented exit-code dictionary; errors that name what failed, where, and the exact command to run instead; Levenshtein-1 flag correction and intent inference; dangerous operations gated and paired with a safe alternative; byte-deterministic output under `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`; `NO_COLOR`/`CI`/non-TTY discipline; no TUI on bare invocation; a regression test pinning every one of these.

All three postures share one **verb registry** (`verbs.toml`): each verb's name, aliases, arguments with types and defaults, required capability, read/write class, output schema id, exit-code mapping, examples, common mistakes, and `next_actions` template. The CLI's subcommands, the daemon's local RPC, and the MCP tools are generated from and checked against that registry (a parity gate fails CI when a verb exists in one surface and not another without a registered reason), so an agent that learned `fa explain balance` already knows the `fa.explain_balance` tool.

### 15.2 Embedded API

```rust
let rt  = fa::runtime_builder().build()?;                   // asupersync runtime, lab-swappable
let book = fa::Book::open(&cx, "acme.fabook", keys)?;       // or Book::create / Book::open_memory
let sess = book.session(&cx, capability_token)?;            // narrowed Cx; capability caveats compiled here

// Reads always name a coordinate; the default is trunk@head, all effective time.
let tb = sess.trial_balance(&cx, Coordinate::default().for_period("2026-03"))?;   // CertifiedReport<TrialBalance>
let b  = sess.balance(&cx, AccountRef::code("1010"), Coordinate::default().known_at(AsOf::Seq(41_999)))?;
let ex = sess.explain_balance(&cx, AccountRef::code("1010"), coord)?;          // legs, entries, evidence heads, proofs

// Writes are intents with a plan first.
let plan = sess.plan(&cx, Intent::RecordInvoice(inv))?;     // Plan { legs, register_deltas, period_decision, sod, certificate }
let out  = sess.commit(&cx, plan)?;                          // Outcome { entry_refs, marker, certificate, next_actions }

// Drafts and scenarios.
let draft = sess.fork_draft(&cx, "close-2026-03-rehearsal")?;
draft.run_close(&cx, PeriodRef::code("2026-03"), CloseMode::DryRun)?;
let diff  = sess.diff(&cx, draft.coordinate(), Coordinate::default())?;      // LedgerDiff { entries, registers, statements }

// Proofs.
let bundle = sess.produce_bundle(&cx, BundleScope::entries(refs).with_evidence())?;
fa_verify::verify(&bundle_bytes, &trusted_checkpoint)?;     // no book needed
```

Every method takes `&Cx`; every read returns a certified value carrying its `Certificate`; every write goes through `plan` → `commit` (a `commit` without a plan is `plan` + `commit` in one call for low-risk intents, refused for the `confirm_plan` classes). GQL is available as `sess.query(&cx, "MATCH …", coord)` with the FrankenAccounting extensions (§15.5).

**Drafts, branches, and scenarios (A6 interim posture, Appendix H row R-BR).** A **draft** is semantically an intent log: a `DraftSet` of planned intents, each validated against the snapshot at planning time, promoted by replaying the intents in one batch at promotion time with full revalidation — which is exactly the substrate's merge-ladder rung 1 (re-evaluate intents against the current head under a stripped `Cx`). When substrate branches ship, a draft may additionally *materialize* on a branch so that registers and statements can be computed over it for review; promotion remains intent replay (the semantic merge), never a storage-level merge of balances. A **scenario** (but-for, pro-forma, policy what-if) is a branch when available and otherwise a deterministic replay of the trunk journal plus scenario intents into a scratch book (`Book::open_memory` is live), which is reproducible by certificate and therefore evidentiary. The coordinate vocabulary is identical in every posture, so no API changes when the substrate lands branches. A draft is *materialized for review* today by replaying trunk-at-`b` plus the draft's intents into an `open_memory` scratch book (the same mechanism as scenarios); statements and `fa close dry-run --draft` run over the scratch book and carry its chain commitment in their certificates (a scratch book has its own `snapshot_identity`; scenario certificates bind it, not trunk's); promotion stays intent replay against trunk under `refresh_snapshot` semantics, each intent's refusal naming the witness that drifted. This is the one law for every draft and scenario read before substrate branches exist (close rehearsal included): a scratch book replays **accounting objects only** (payload and lifecycle objects; never access events, checkpoints, or maintenance records) and says so in its certificates (`scope: accounting_objects_only`); identities and digests are preserved because vids are identity-derived and payloads exclude recorded time (§4.2, §4.9), so approvals bound to proposal digests remain valid across replay; the scratch book's commit sequences differ, so its certificate carries a `SeqMap` (trunk `CommitSeq` → scratch `CommitSeq`) and binds `source_trunk_checkpoint` plus the scratch `snapshot_identity`; a scratch book never forges recorded time and never claims original membership — a promotion that lands on trunk is the entry's only membership.

### 15.3 The `fa` CLI

**Invocation shape.** `fa <verb> [subverb] [args] [--book <dir>] [--as-known-at …] [--for-period …] [--branch …] [--json | --robot] [--dry-run]`. Bare `fa` prints a one-screen triage (book detected or not, daemon status, what needs attention, the five most useful next commands) and exits 0; it never opens a TUI. `fa --help` and `fa help <verb>` mention `--json`, `capabilities`, `robot-docs`, and the exit-code dictionary in their footer.

**Verb taxonomy (1.0).**

| Family | Verbs |
|---|---|
| Orientation | `fa` (triage), `fa triage --json`, `fa doctor`, `fa capabilities --json`, `fa robot-docs guide|schema|exit-codes|examples`, `fa next` (what to do next, from open findings, unmatched items, pending approvals, period state) |
| Book lifecycle | `fa book create|open|info|fork|checkpoint|verify`, `fa migrate` |
| Chart and registries | `fa chart list|show|import|open-account|close-account|promote`, `fa unit list|add`, `fa law list|show`, `fa calendar show|periods`, `fa rule propose|backtest` |
| Posting | `fa post --intent <file|json>` (any intent), `fa invoice|bill|receipt|payment|accrue|defer|reclass|reverse|replace|allocate|revalue|reserve|post-reserve|void-reserve` (typed sugar over `post`; two-phase entries are *reserves* on the CLI so that `fa hold` means only the governance hold), `fa batch` |
| Explain | `fa explain balance <acct>`, `fa explain entry <ref>`, `fa explain lineage <ref>`, `fa explain evidence <ref>`, `fa explain period <code>`, `fa explain register <cell>`, `fa explain refusal <refusal_id>` |
| Query | `fa query "<GQL>"`, `fa gl detail`, `fa tb`, `fa statement <definition>`, `fa statement lint <definition>`, `fa aging ar|ap`, `fa open-items` |
| Time and close | `fa period list|open|soft-close|close|lock|reopen`, `fa close dry-run|run|status`, `fa diff --from … --to …` |
| Restatement | `fa case open|rehearse|approve|post|disclose|show|diff` |
| Evidence | `fa doc import|show|attach|list`, `fa attest`, `fa assert-balance`, `fa bank import|match|confirm|status`, `fa recon book-to-book` |
| Forensics | `fa procedure list|run <id>`, `fa finding list|show|review|dismiss|export`, `fa exhibit mark`, `fa workpaper export`, `fa trace <law> …`, `fa jet`, `fa benford`, `fa sample mus|random` |
| Drafts and scenarios | `fa draft new|list|show|plan|promote|discard`, `fa scenario fork|run|diff`, `fa branch list` |
| Proofs | `fa bundle produce|show`, `fa verify bundle|registers|book|certificate|close|fx-conversion|projections|periods`, `fa checkpoint list|sign|witness`, `fa certificate status <certificate>`, `fa replay <certificate>` |
| Governance | `fa cap issue|list|revoke|show`, `fa hold place|release`, `fa erase` (dual control), `fa access log` |
| Interop | `fa export ads|saft|xbrl|beancount|csv`, `fa import beancount|csv|qbo|camt|bai2|ofx`, `fa recon book-to-book` |
| Daemon | `fad serve|status|stop` (the only long-running process; `fa` auto-routes to it when present) |
| Sandbox | `fa demo init --scenario <name> --seed <n>`, `fa gen book …` |

**Robot mode.** `--robot` (or `--json`) switches every verb to line-oriented NDJSON on stdout with stderr reserved for diagnostics; every line is an envelope `{"v": 1, "event": "<schema id>", "book": …, "coordinate": …, "data": …, "certificate": …}` — `event` is the discriminator exactly as in `fgdb`'s robot mode, and `kind` is reserved for result kinds — with a stable, versioned schema that `fa robot-docs schema <event>` prints and that a regression test pins from `registries/robot_schema/`. `--robot` is accepted in any position and as the first argument (the `fgdb` convention); every non-zero exit emits exactly one `error` event carrying `refusal_id`, `exit`, and `class`, which is what the never-silent-fail regression test asserts. Ordering is canonical (sorted keys, sorted collections); timestamps appear only in fields; `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` pins any time the tool would otherwise read; `NO_COLOR`, `CI=true`, `TERM=dumb`, and a non-TTY stdout suppress all styling; no verb ever prompts in non-TTY. Human mode is the default when stdout is a TTY and is plain text tables.

**Exit-code dictionary** (`fa robot-docs exit-codes`; a strict superset of `fgdb`'s so an agent that uses both tools learns one table — §0.4 decision 8): `0` success · `2` usage or input error (bad flag, malformed intent, unknown code — with the exact fix; `= fgdb usage`) · `3` accounting-law or query refusal (unbalanced, unit mismatch, missing required dimension, a query the profile rejects — the law and the cell cited; `= fgdb query`; an *inadmissible class for the period's state* is a safety block, exit `7`, not `3`) · `4` environment or open failure (no book, daemon unreachable, key file missing or wrong permissions, toolchain; `= fgdb open`) · `5` foundation or I/O failure (substrate error, corruption detected, `RecoveryRequired` — fail closed, never partial, with `fa doctor` guidance; `= fgdb io`) · `6` conflict (witness-validation abort after the configured retries, idempotency conflict, `PlanInvalidated`) · `7` safety block (period law, SoD, capability, `confirm_plan` missing, destructive without `--yes`) · `8` verification failure (a proof, bundle, register fold, or certificate replay did not verify). `1` is never emitted. `fa … --json` with no results exits `0` with `data: []`.

**Mega-commands.** `fa triage --json` returns `{health, period_states, unbalanced_drafts, pending_approvals, unmatched_bank_lines, open_findings_by_severity, register_watermark_lag, next_actions: [exact commands]}` in one call. `fa explain balance <acct> --for-period P --as-known-at T --json` returns the register cell, the fold proof, every contributing entry with its evidence heads, and the known-at history of that cell (when the balance last changed and why). `fa next --json` ranks the next actions for the caller's capability. `fa doctor --json` checks book directory, key file, daemon, writer lease, register watermarks, accumulator continuity, witness freshness, and registry epochs.

**Errors that teach.** Every refusal carries `{code, what_failed, where: {entry|leg|field}, why: law or policy cited with registry id, fix: exact command or field change, alternatives: [safe commands], docs: robot-docs anchor}`. Examples from the catalog (Appendix D): posting to a summary account → the leaf children listed with the nearest by name; a float in an amount field → refused with the exact integer-mantissa form and the unit's scale; an effective date in a closed period → the period's state, the admissible classes, the capability needed, and the reclass-into-next-period alternative; unbalanced → the per-unit imbalance, the suggested residual leg if within one minor unit and a rounding account exists (shown, never applied); `--jsno` → `did you mean --json`; `fa post` with legs that look like a reversal of an existing entry → "this looks like a reversal of E…; use `fa reverse E… --reason …` so lineage is recorded". Refusals are themselves addressable (`refusal_id`) so `fa explain refusal` can expand one later and an agent can cite it.

**Intent inference.** Levenshtein-1 correction on flags and verbs (the known-flag list is extracted from `verbs.toml`, not hand-maintained); aliases for the spellings agents try first (`fa trial-balance` = `fa tb`, `fa gl` = `fa gl detail`, `fa balance` = `fa explain balance`, `fa journal` = `fa post`); mis-ordered coordinate flags accepted anywhere; `fa post --dry-run` implied for Agent-profile capabilities (the tool says so once per session); an instant given where a civil date is expected is **refused** (FA-REF-100, exit `2`) with the civil date the book's `time_zone_policy` would derive, so the agent chooses explicitly — never a silent conversion. Correction may *suggest* but never *execute* a write-class verb: `fa psot …` exits `2` with the corrected command, while `fa tb --jsno` runs with a warning. `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` affects only display fields (`MANIFEST.md`, human tables), because no data path reads a clock (§7.1a). Without a running `fad`, `fa triage` opens a read view (`open_read_view`) and reports `daemon: absent` rather than failing.

### 15.4 The MCP server

`fa-mcp` (over fastmcp_rust) is hosted by `fad` and exposes the verb registry as tool clusters of at most seven tools each, with resources for discovery and macros for the workflows agents actually run:

| Cluster | Tools |
|---|---|
| `fa.orient` | `triage`, `doctor`, `next`, `capabilities`, `robot_docs` |
| `fa.book` | `create`, `open`, `info`, `checkpoint`, `verify`, `migrate` |
| `fa.chart` | `list_accounts`, `show_account`, `import_chart`, `open_account`, `close_account`, `promote_to_summary`, `list_units_laws_periods` |
| `fa.govern` | `issue_capability`, `revoke_capability`, `list_capabilities`, `place_hold`, `release_hold`, `access_log`, `erase` |
| `fa.io` | `export_ads`, `export_saft`, `export_xbrl`, `export_beancount`, `export_csv`, `import_document_batch`, `import_ledger_text` |
| `fa.post` | `plan_intent`, `commit_plan`, `post_intent` (plan+commit for low-risk classes), `post_batch`, `explain_refusal` |
| `fa.read` | `balance`, `explain_balance`, `trial_balance`, `statement`, `gl_detail`, `query_gql`, `open_items` |
| `fa.time` | `period_status`, `period_transition`, `close_dry_run`, `close_run`, `diff_coordinates` |
| `fa.evidence` | `import_document`, `attach_evidence`, `attest`, `assert_balance`, `bank_import`, `bank_match`, `confirm_match` |
| `fa.forensics` | `run_procedure`, `list_findings`, `review_finding`, `trace`, `sample`, `export_workpaper` |
| `fa.draft` | `fork_draft`, `plan_in_draft`, `promote_draft`, `discard_draft`, `fork_scenario`, `diff_branches` |
| `fa.notary` | `produce_bundle`, `verify_bundle`, `replay_certificate`, `sign_checkpoint`, `list_checkpoints` |
| `fa.case` | `open_case`, `rehearse_case`, `approve_case`, `post_case`, `disclose_case` |

Resources: `resource://fa/book`, `resource://fa/chart/{book}`, `resource://fa/periods/{book}`, `resource://fa/entry/{ref}`, `resource://fa/lineage/{ref}`, `resource://fa/evidence/{ref}`, `resource://fa/findings/{book}?status=open`, `resource://fa/drafts/{principal}`, `resource://fa/capabilities/{token}`, `resource://fa/laws`, `resource://fa/intents` (the intent schemas), `resource://fa/exit-codes`. Macros: `macro_record_with_evidence` (import document → record event → plan → commit → attach), `macro_month_end_dry_run` (fork draft → run close → statements → diff → summary), `macro_investigate_party` (related-party closure → flows → JET on the party's entries → findings → workpaper), `macro_reconcile_instrument` (import statement → auto-match → list unmatched → assert balance). Every tool returns `next_actions`, stable handles, and a certificate id; the mapping from `verbs.toml` to tools is total — every CLI verb maps to a tool, with exactly two registered exclusions (`fad serve|status|stop`, which control the host process, and `fa demo init`, which writes outside a book) — and at most 40 tools are exposed by default (many hosts degrade past that; the six core clusters `fa.orient`, `fa.book`, `fa.chart`, `fa.post`, `fa.read`, and `fa.time` expose 35 tools by default, while `fa.evidence`, `fa.draft`, `fa.forensics`, `fa.case`, `fa.notary`, `fa.govern`, and `fa.io` are discoverable on-demand through `fa.orient.capabilities` or a `--profile`); the parity gate compares the JSON schemas generated from `verbs.toml` with fastmcp's `Tool.input_schema`, not just names; amounts cross the MCP boundary only as strings (`"1234.56 USD"`), never as JSON numbers; every error is `{type, message, recoverable, data: {fix_hint, suggested_tool_calls, available_options}}`; mistake detectors catch floats, instants where dates are expected, summary accounts, closed periods, agents calling `approve` on their own draft, placeholder values (`YOUR_BOOK`), and broadcast-style scopes ("all accounts" on a write). Tool documentation follows the Do/Don't/Discovery/Examples/Common-mistakes template verbatim, generated from `verbs.toml`. A fake-CLI stub is not needed because the real CLI exists and routes to the same daemon; the MCP server's `robot_docs` explains both.

### 15.5 Query language

GQL (through the substrate) plus a namespaced FrankenAccounting profile: the profile is a **rewriter**, not an engine extension — `fa-query` compiles profile text to the native statements `Database::query` accepts (`FOR PERIOD P` ⇒ a join on the entry's `IN_PERIOD` edge to the period coded `P`, the period its posting resolved, so an adjustment period's entries never count in the normal period that shares its days, while `EFFECTIVE BETWEEN` bounds the leg's effective day and `EFFECTIVE AT d` keeps every leg effective on or before `d` (the stock `fa tb --as-of d` reads), each an `EffectiveSelector` the coordinate names; `AS KNOWN AT` ⇒ `FOR SYSTEM_TIME AS OF SEQ $seq` with the seq resolved per §7.1a; `AT BRANCH` ⇒ the host branch resolver, trunk only until R-BR, or a scenario scratch book; `CALL fa.<proc>` ⇒ executed in `fa-lantern`/`fa-statements` over the certified native result, admitted anywhere in the pipeline when the procedure is a pure per-row function such as `fa.open_amount`, and otherwise only as the first or last clause — each emitting its own certificate that wraps the substrate's `NativeResultCertificate`); `fa-conformance` pins the corpus of native statements the rewriter may emit, and only `Database::query` is targeted (§0.4 decision 10). The profile type-checks amounts: `sum`, `avg`, and comparison over `Amount` values of different units are refused at rewrite time (`FA-REF-189` QUERY_AMOUNT_LAW; the native read sums no decimal, so the profile computes amount aggregates exactly over the certified native rows), so an aggregate is always grouped by unit or explicitly converted under a named law. The profile adds the selectors of §7.4 as clauses (`FOR PERIOD`, `AS KNOWN AT`, `EFFECTIVE AT|BETWEEN`, `AT BRANCH`, `AS ORIGINALLY REPORTED`, `AS CURRENTLY STATED`, `AS REPORTED IN`), procedures as `CALL fa.<procedure>(…) YIELD …` (every §11 procedure, `fa.trial_balance`, `fa.statement`, `fa.explain_balance`, `fa.fold`), and `EXPLAIN (CERTIFICATE)` inherited. Examples:

```gql
-- Receivables over 90 days from related parties, as the books stood at fieldwork start
MATCH (l:Leg)-[:POSTS_TO]->(a:Account {code: '1200'})<-[:CONTROLS]-(:Subledger {kind:'AR'}),
      (l)-[:FOR]->(:DimensionValue)<-[:REPRESENTS]-(p:Party), (l)-[:IN_UNIT]->(u:Unit)
WHERE EXISTS { MATCH (p)-[:RELATED_TO|OWNS|CONTROLS*1..3]-(:LegalEntity {code:'ACME'}) EFFECTIVE AT l.effective_day }
FOR PERIOD 'FY2025' AS KNOWN AT '2026-02-01T00:00:00Z'
CALL fa.open_amount(l) YIELD open, age_days
WHERE age_days > 90
RETURN p.name, u.code, sum(open) AS exposure ORDER BY exposure DESC;   -- sums are per unit; the profile refuses a sum over mixed units

-- Every entry whose restatement lineage touched Q4, with the net effect
MATCH (e:Entry)-[s:SUPERSEDES {kind:'Restates'}]->(t:Entry) FOR PERIOD 'FY2024-Q4'
CALL fa.lineage_net(t) YIELD net_legs RETURN t, e, s.reason_code, net_legs;

-- Follow the money six hops out from a party within a window, by LIBR
CALL fa.trace('libr', {source: $party, from: '2025-01-01', to: '2025-06-30', max_hops: 6})
YIELD instrument, period, traced_amount, path RETURN *;
```

### 15.6 Worked workflows (the five readers)

**The agent closes a month.** `fa triage --json` → unmatched bank lines on instrument B1, two accrual tasks pending, period 2026-03 `Open`. `fa draft new close-03` → `fa bank import stmt.camt053.xml --instrument B1 --draft close-03` → `fa bank match --draft close-03 --json` (18 exact, 2 proposals) → `fa bank confirm M-7 M-9 --draft close-03` (refused: Agent profile cannot confirm heuristic matches; `fix: ask an approver to run fa bank confirm …`, logged) → `fa accrue --intent accrue_rent.json --draft close-03 --dry-run` (plan: legs, period decision `Open/Adjusting → Admit`) → `fa draft plan close-03 --json` (all plans valid) → `fa close dry-run --period 2026-03 --draft close-03` (statements, diff vs February, warnings: one round-amount manual entry) → `fa draft promote close-03` (refused `7`: promotion requires Approver; `next_actions: [fa draft show close-03 --json]` for the human) → the controller runs `fa draft promote close-03 --yes` → `fa period soft-close 2026-03`. Every refusal taught the next step; nothing touched trunk until a human did.

**The CPA audits FY2025.** The firm receives an Auditor capability pinned `known_at ≤ <engagement start seq>`. `fa export ads --period FY2025 --with-proofs` yields the GL detail in the AICPA layout with the accumulator checkpoint and range proofs; `fa verify bundle` on the firm's laptop confirms completeness. `fa jet --period FY2025 --json` and `fa benford --scope expenses` produce findings; `fa sample mus --population ar-open --seed 20260115 --interval 50000` yields a reproducible sample; `fa explain evidence E…` for each sampled item pulls the invoice and receipt documents; `fa assert-balance` records the bank confirmation; at year-end the firm signs the checkpoint as a witness (`fa checkpoint witness`) — the signature attests only "this root was observed at this time", never the ledger's content, so it creates no bookkeeping or management-function involvement under the independence rules (Regulation S-X Rule 2-01; AICPA ET 1.295). The client cannot alter FY2025 history during fieldwork without the firm's witnessed root exposing it.

**The FDIC examiner needs close-of-business positions.** `fa tb --effective-at 2026-04-10 --json` gives the positions by ledger date; `--as-known-at <seq>` adds what the bank's systems knew at that instant — the receiver wants the first, the examiner of controls the second, and they are different questions. Part 370 is a registered **profile**, not a trial balance: deposit `Instrument { kind: Deposit }` liabilities carrying ownership-right-and-capacity codes, `Party` participants (owners, beneficiaries, agents) as role-typed edges, aggregation per owner across accounts and capacities, provisional holds as `Pending` entries under the `receivership` capability, and `fa-formats` writers for the rule's prescribed output files (customer, account, account-participant, pending) at the end-of-day effective date — inside the 24-hour window the rule names. Every read is logged under the examiner's capability; `fa replay` reproduces each figure later.

**The trustee or receiver takes over.** `fa book fork receivership --at <appointment seq>` plus `fa hold place --scope book --authority '<order>'`: the pre-appointment trunk is locked under hold, the estate posts on the receivership branch (a scratch-book replay until substrate branches land) under the `Receiver` profile, pre-petition and post-petition are a branch boundary rather than a date filter, and the "as of appointment" statements are a certificate the court receives.

**The litigation consultant computes but-for damages.** `fa scenario fork but-for-breach --from 2025-07-01` → apply the counterfactual intents (the contract performed; the diverted receipts recorded) → `fa statement income --branch but-for-breach --for-period FY2025` vs trunk → `fa scenario diff` is the damages schedule with a certificate naming every assumption document; `fa bundle produce --scope scenario:but-for-breach --redaction privileged-v2` is the production, hash-certified; opposing counsel runs `fa-verify` and `fa replay` on the same bundle.

**The investigator traces funds.** `fa procedure run graph.related_party_exposure --entity ACME --json`, `fa procedure run graph.layering --window 2025 --json`, `fa trace libr --source P-412 --from 2025-01-01 --to 2025-12-31 --max-hops 8 --json` → findings with cited `BankLine`s and legs → `fa hold place --scope party:P-412 --authority 'GJ subpoena 26-114'` → `fa finding export --format md` as the workpaper; `fa access log --since …` shows the investigator's own trail for the privilege log.

### 15.7 The human surface

Human mode is plain-text tables that fit a terminal, with the same content as robot mode; `--table`, `--wide`, and `--csv` are formatting options. There is no TUI in 1.0 by product choice; `ftui` is eligible, and a `--watch` inline view is a registered post-1.0 scope decision. Web dashboards are downstream consumers of the robot contract.

### 15.8 Sandbox and fixtures

`fa demo init --scenario retail|bank|ponzi|shell-network|worldcom --seed 7` creates a fully populated book from `fa-gen` with planted facts documented in `README.md` of the demo directory, so an agent can practice every verb, every procedure, and every refusal on a realistic book that cannot hurt anyone; the scenarios are the same corpora Lantern's precision/recall is measured on (§11.10).

### 15.9 What an agent wants, mapped

| Want | Feature |
|---|---|
| One call that says what needs attention | `fa triage` / `fa.orient.triage` |
| To be unable to corrupt the books by accident | Agent profile: branch-confined, dry-run required, ceilings; period law; SoD at commit |
| Errors that say exactly what to do | Refusal catalog with `fix` and `alternatives`; `fa explain refusal` |
| Stable IDs to cite | `EntryRef{vid, digest}` and every handle in §4.9 |
| "Why is this balance X" | `fa explain balance` with fold proof and evidence heads |
| Deterministic output to diff | Canonical ordering, `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, certificates |
| Typed intents, with raw legs when needed | Intent catalog + `PostEntry` |
| Evidence attached in the same call | `macro_record_with_evidence`; `evidence` on every intent |
| Bulk and idempotent | `PostBatch`, idempotency keys, outcomes durable before ack |
| A query language it already knows | GQL + the small FrankenAccounting profile |
| Discoverable contract in-tool | `capabilities --json`, `robot-docs`, `resource://fa/intents` |
| A sandbox | `fa demo init` |
| What-if without consequences | Drafts and scenarios |
| Safe, time-boxed access | Caveated capabilities with expiry |
| Reasons for refusals that cite policy | Every refusal names the registry row |
| Streaming for big extracts | NDJSON rows; `fa gl detail --stream` |
| No TUI, no silent failure, no prompts in non-TTY | Polish Bar, regression-tested |
| An audit-ready artifact in one command | `fa bundle produce`, `fa workpaper export` |

---

## 16. Determinism, Evidence Classes, and Verification Doctrine (Bet A5, part 2)

This is not a QA appendix; it is a design pillar with its own budget, inherited from the substrate's §15 and specialized to accounting.

### 16.1 Claim classes and registries

The substrate's lattice (`invariant > proof > bounded_model > statistical > slo > benchmark`) applies verbatim through `fgdb-claim`'s types, so an illegal justification is a compile error. `registries/invariants.toml` holds exactly the FA-INV IDs of Appendix F, each materialized as clauses with `checker_entrypoint`, `negative_test_entrypoint`, `owner`, `first_gate`, `status`, and `waiver = "forbidden"`; `evidence.toml` holds every Lantern detector's evidence row and every calibration row; `slo.toml` holds every §17 number. `claims_lint.toml` scans this plan, the README, and AGENTS.md both ways: every `FA-XXX-NN` marker resolves to a row, and every gate-table region cites a marker. `tools/registry-check` (std-only, like the substrate's) validates all of it and is itself a G0 deliverable.

### 16.2 The reference oracle (`fa-reference`)

A deliberately simple, single-threaded, obviously correct implementation of the full accounting semantics over canonical maps and vectors: posting with the balance law, period law, admissibility matrix, SoD, idempotency; registers as literal folds; bitemporal selectors as filters over `(effective_date, recorded_seq)`; supersession netting; two-phase resolution; escrow bounds as a check after every operation; allocation and FX under the laws; statement definitions evaluated by brute force; every Lantern procedure in its naïve form; the accumulator as a literal Merkle tree. Compiled for tests, fuzzing, and model checking only; never shipped; never optimized. Its dependency allowlist is `std`, `fa-time`, and `fa-registry` (the generated registries) — importing `fa-money`, `fgdb`, `fa-journal`, or any engine crate is a CI-rejected boundary violation (§6.1, §18.1), so the differential cannot be gutted by code sharing. The normative appendices and golden vectors outrank both implementations: a mismatch is triaged against the spec and never resolved by making the reference imitate the engine. **It exists before the first engine line**, which is also what lets FrankenAccounting's semantics be developed while the substrate's planned surfaces land (§2.5).

### 16.3 External oracles

- **Plain-text accounting.** `fa export beancount` and `fa export ledger` produce journals that `bean-check`, `bean-report`, `hledger balance`, and `ledger balance` evaluate; balances are compared cell-by-cell for every fixture book, including multi-currency and lot-tracked fixtures. Divergences are triaged against both specs (Beancount's rounding and price semantics differ by design; the documented divergence table is itself a conformance artifact). These tools are widely used, open, and independently implemented — a two-hop oracle to ground truth for the fold.
- **TigerBeetle semantic corpus.** A scenario corpus of two-leg, two-phase, linked, balancing, closing, and limit cases expressed in both TigerBeetle's API vocabulary and FrankenAccounting intents, with expected balance fields; FrankenAccounting must agree with TigerBeetle's documented semantics where the compat profile claims parity and document divergence elsewhere (N-leg entries, expiry sweeps).
- **AICPA ADS and SAF-T round trips.** Export → the published validators (where available) and schema checks → re-import into a fresh book → fold equality with proofs.
- **XBRL.** Exported instances validate against the taxonomy's calculation linkbase; a failed calculation check is a defect in a statement definition and fails CI.
- **`fa-verify`** as the independent verifier of the producer's bundles (independence discipline: a different crate with its own BLAKE3 and decoders).
- **Public-record forensic case studies.** Documented histories from public filings and investigation reports (WorldCom's line-cost capitalization, Enron's restatements, Lehman's Repo 105 balances, the BLMIS trustee's Exhibit B ledgers, XBRL-tagged restatements, a merger's purchase-price allocation) are replayed as end-to-end tests: a case model in `fa-oracles` and a runner in `crates/fa-cli/tests/cases.rs`. Every fact carries its provenance; a published tie-out is an exact observation on the constructed book, never a proof; a detection or refusal is stated for each encoding the record leaves open; and every case renders what it cannot show. The normative plan is `docs/FORENSIC_CASE_STUDIES_PLAN.md`, and its work items are beads labelled `case-studies`.
- **frankenscipy's `fsci_stats`** as a test-only differential oracle for Benford and sampling statistics — a registered *test-only* dependency, outside every posture closure, listed in `deny.toml`'s dev-dependency allowlist exactly like the external `hledger`/`bean-check` binaries the oracle harness shells out to (§18.4).
- **The substrate behavior suite** (`fa-conformance/substrate/`): one test per relied-upon substrate behavior — a point-read witness conflicts with a concurrent property write in both orders; `SnapshotAdvanced` then `refresh_snapshot`; `delta_since` is gap-free and `DeltaCursorRetired` triggers a rebuild; `create_vertex` refuses `AlreadyLive`; `execute_certified` then `replay` is byte-identical; `open_memory` parity with a directory book; `write_ordered` is one publication under `finish_with_crash` at every `CrashPoint`; and the call-path probes the audit asked for — post-vs-close in both orders, an entry into an empty period (the witness is the `Period` vertex, not a scan), concurrent double application of one receipt, a limited-account race through the `LimitCell` CAS. It runs on every `franken-stack.lock` bump and is the executable form of Appendix H's drift check.

### 16.4 Simulation-first (`fa-sim`)

The entire system — Folio, the register maintainer, the expiry sweep, Canon, Lantern, Notary, `fad` with its MCP and local transports — runs under the asupersync lab runtime with the substrate's lab VFS: seeded deterministic scheduling, virtual time, torn writes, bit flips, ENOSPC, fsync lies, crash points at every barrier of the posting pipeline (between plan and commit; between commit and ack; between delta consumption and watermark publication; between checkpoint computation and checkpoint publication; between case approval and case post; between bundle production and bundle hand-off), and DPOR over the declared interleavings that matter: post vs close, two agents applying the same receipt, promotion vs a trunk post to the same account, a restatement vs a reopen, an expiry sweep vs a manual void. Every failure is a seed with a crashpack and a replay command; failing runs shrink themselves (delta debugging inherited). Lab-vs-live dual runs compare traces for the Genesis slice scenarios.

### 16.5 Consistency oracles (in-sim, continuous)

After every committed transaction in a lab run: balance law on every entry; register-equals-fold on every touched cell; subledger-equals-control; admissibility of every entry for its period's state at commit; SoD on every approval; idempotency outcome stability; supersession acyclicity and exact negation of reversals; accumulator consistency (each root extends the previous); escrow conservation (rights + custody = capacity); bitemporal monotonicity (a known-at view never changes once its seq is passed); projection-equals-payload for legs; erasure leaves amounts intact and downgrades grades attributably. Oracles are implemented against the reference engine, not against the engine's own types, wherever practical.

### 16.6 Differential, metamorphic, and property suites

Model-generated intent histories run against both engines and compare every outcome, register, statement, and finding. Metamorphic laws: the fold is invariant under reordering of commutative entries within a period; reversal-then-replacement equals replacement for every register; posting on a draft and promoting equals posting on trunk; a scenario replay equals a branch when both exist; export-then-import is identity on folds; a register rebuilt from the journal equals the maintained register; a bundle produced twice is byte-identical; compaction, checkpointing, and retention cuts never change any certified result inside the retained range; redaction never changes an amount; calendar generation agrees with a brute-force day-walker. Property suites for Abacus (§6.7), for calendar arithmetic (exhaustive over 1900–2100), for the admissibility matrix (exhaustive), and for the refusal catalog (every refusal has a `fix` that, applied, succeeds on the fixture).

### 16.7 Formal anchors (scoped, honest)

Lean: `Amount` group laws; rounding bounds per law; allocation conservation per law; balance-law preservation under reversal, replacement, and reclassification; supersession-net correctness; bitemporal visibility (an entry is visible at `(known_at, effective)` iff its `recorded_seq ≤ known_at` and its `effective_date ∈ effective`); the period state machine's reachability (no path from `Locked` to `Open` without a `Reopen` control entry carrying a case); escrow bound preservation under the registered transitions. TLA+/TLC: the close run as a DAG under crashes; the restatement case life cycle with dual control; the two-phase hold with expiry sweep and concurrent manual resolution; draft promotion against a moving trunk. Every model states its bounds; trace export demonstrates captured executions are allowed by the spec; none of this is called a proof of the implementation.

### 16.8 Fuzzing

Every `fa-formats` reader (camt, BAI2, OFX, MT940, CSV layouts, Beancount/Ledger text, ADS, SAF-T, XBRL instance), the Canon parser and IR decoder, the bundle decoder, the certificate decoder, the robot-mode request parsers, and the MCP argument normalizers — each with a corpus and a crash/timeout/memory budget in CI.

### 16.9 The gauntlet

Before every release, the convergent multi-round honest-evaluation gauntlet (the suite's `running-the-gauntlet` discipline) runs: oracle and differential harnesses, metamorphic suites, surface-parity audit (CLI vs MCP vs API against `verbs.toml`), the agent-ergonomics Polish Bar scorecard with fresh-context simulated agents attempting the canonical tasks of §15.6, honest performance versus the reference machine's pinned baselines, and a release-readiness scorecard whose every row names its evidence class.

### 16.10 Publish

Target four papers or tech reports: (1) *The Palimpsest Ledger: bitemporal restatement semantics for immutable books*; (2) *Balance = Fold: certified registers over a content-addressed journal*; (3) *Tracing as code: reproducible constructive-trust tracing and Ponzi-flow detection on a temporal money graph*; (4) *Agent-operated books: typed intents, dry-run plans, and structural segregation of duties*. Ambition includes the receipts.

---

## 17. Performance Doctrine and Targets

Reference machine: 32-core/64-thread, 256 GB RAM, PCIe-4 NVMe, single node. Every number is a provisional `EmpiricalGate` (`slo.toml` row), activated only when its benchmark manifest pins CPU, kernel/filesystem/mount, NVMe model and measured fsync distribution, toolchain, substrate revision, book configuration (retention, FEC, encryption, register definitions), fixture (an `fa-gen` book by seed), concurrency, result consumption, and comparison rule. Every gate names its operation class (Appendix G) and must be derivable from the substrate's barrier chain for that class: a posting-latency gate below `barrier_count × pinned fsync p50` is a registry error that fails CI. The standing laws are the substrate's six (no benchmark-only semantics; distributions not averages; never hide compaction or register maintenance; memory is a metric; adaptive numbers disclose their policy; no unpriced protocol weight) plus one of our own: **no "balance read" number is published without stating whether it was served at the watermark or with a tail fold, and the tail length.**

| Domain | Provisional gate |
|---|---|
| Single-entry post (one publication, two ordered syncs, with projections, 2–6 legs) | p50 ≤ 2 × the pinned fsync p50; p99 < 15 ms warm; the sustained *unbatched* rate is bounded above by `1 / (2 × fsync p50)` by derivability on today's single-writer handle and is reported, never gated, until group commit lands (Appendix H R-GC) |
| Batched post (`PostBatch`, N ≤ `max_batch_entries`) | ≥ 20k entries/s sustained at N = 100 on one writer with full durability, encryption, and FEC on; batch p99 < 60 ms |
| Bulk migration (50M legacy lines) | ≥ 500k legs/s into sealed storage via the substrate bulk protocol; opening-balance attestation computed in the same run |
| Balance read at watermark (`SnapshotQuery` class) | p99 < 50 µs warm; tail fold ≤ 1 µs per tail leg, tail length reported |
| Trial balance, 100k leaf accounts, at watermark | < 200 ms; certified against fold |
| Full register rebuild from journal (10M legs) | < 10 s on the reference machine; digest-equal to maintained registers |
| Register watermark lag under sustained post gate | p99 < 250 ms behind head; lag is a reported `slo` row, never an invariant |
| Known-at trial balance (any retained seq) | < 2.5× the head trial balance for the pinned retention fixture |
| Close dry-run (1M-entry period, 40 tasks) | < 30 s to statements and diff |
| Restatement diff certificate (3 periods, 200 statement lines) | < 5 s |
| Bundle production (10k entries with evidence heads and proofs) | < 5 s; `fa-verify` of the same bundle < 2 s on a laptop class machine; per-entry inclusion proof verify < 100 µs |
| Lantern: JET suite over 10M legs | < 20 s total; Benford first-two-digits < 3 s; duplicates < 10 s |
| Lantern: `graph.layering` over a 1M-party, 20M-flow projection, 6 hops | < 60 s with CGSE witness; `trace.libr` over 1 instrument-year < 1 s |
| Consolidation (20 entities, 2M entries each) on a branch | < 2 min to certified consolidated statements |
| Accumulator checkpoint | O(log n) peaks; signing < 10 ms; witness round-trip reported, never gated |
| CLI first-try latency (`fa triage --json`, warm daemon) | < 100 ms; bare `fa` with no book < 50 ms |
| MCP tool round-trip (read-class) | < 20 ms over stdio excluding the read itself |
| Memory: bytes per live leg (payload + projections + indexes + registers + accumulator) | reported with component breakdown; provisional ceiling 400 B/leg at steady state |
| Endurance: 24 h sustained post at 50% of gate | no unexplained RSS drift; register lag bounded; compaction debt bounded in the substrate's model terms |

Method: every gate has a bench binary, a committed baseline, a variance budget, and a flamegraph on regression; the harness is deterministic (seeded `fa-gen` workloads, pinned schedules). Performance work follows the house discipline: profile → remove one contention or allocation → re-verify determinism and the oracles → commit with evidence, one lever per change.

---

## 18. Workspace Layout and the Build-It-Ourselves Inventory

### 18.1 Crates (`fa-*` workspace, layered; Cargo-enforced boundaries per the substrate's `workspace_topology.toml` pattern)

| Layer | Crates |
|---|---|
| Foundation | `fa-types` (ids, handles, canonical encodings, claim re-exports), `fa-money` (Abacus), `fa-time` (Almanac calendars and selectors), `fa-registry` (generated registry readers), `fa-claim` (thin re-export of `fgdb-claim` with FA ids), `fa-sim-harness` (the lab-runtime harness every later crate's tests run under — present before the first kernel line) |
| Model | `fa-model` (REA schema, symbols, entry classes, admissibility, labels/edges as typed builders, control payloads) |
| Laws (pure; no I/O; stripped `Cx`) | `fa-canon` (language, type checker, IR, interim evaluator, VM adapter), `fa-policy` (caveat evaluation, SoD policy evaluation, ceilings, evidence-minimum policy), `fa-intents` (intent schemas and plan types) |
| Kernel | `fa-journal` (Folio posting kernel: calls *down* into Laws for rule evaluation and authorization; write path; projections), `fa-registers` (maintainer, checkpoints, folds), `fa-escrow` (balance-limit and usage reservation cells), `fa-palimpsest` (supersession, netting, case and lifecycle state machines — produces no reports) |
| Evidence | `fa-docket` (documents, custody, attestations, bank lines, erasable fields), `fa-notary` (accumulator maintainer in commit order, checkpoints, scope index, bundles, certificates) |
| Analytics | `fa-statements` (definitions, report engine, restatement diffs over two coordinates), `fa-concordat` (multi-book, intercompany, consolidation, FX translation), `fa-lantern` (procedures, findings, tracing laws, projections), `fa-query` (the GQL profile rewriter) |
| Governance | `fa-steward` (capability issuance, profiles, access audit — evaluation lives in `fa-policy` below the kernel) |
| Interop | `fa-formats` (ADS, SAF-T, XBRL, camt/pain, BAI2, OFX, MT940, CSV, QBO, Beancount/Ledger, load files) |
| Surface | `fa-verbs` (the verb registry and parity checker), `fa` (embedded API facade and the *orchestrator* of multi-crate workflows such as case rehearsal = statements over a scratch book + diff), `fa-demo` (production-safe synthetic-book generator for `fa demo init`; no dependency on the reference oracle), `fa-cli` (`fa` binary), `fad` (daemon: writer lease, local RPC, hosts MCP), `fa-mcp` (fastmcp adapter) |
| Verification | `fa-reference` (own limb arithmetic; no engine crate), `fa-sim` (scenarios over `fa-sim-harness`), `fa-oracles`, `fa-gen` (labeled fraud corpora; may depend on `fa-demo`), `fa-bench`, `fa-conformance`, `fa-fuzz`, `fa-verify` (std-only; outside every posture closure except its own) |
| Tooling | `tools/registry-check` (std-only), `tools/xtask` |

Rules: a crate may depend only on its own or lower layers, and the layering above is a legal DAG by construction — the kernel reaches rule evaluation and authorization by calling *down* into the Laws layer, Palimpsest never calls Statements (the facade orchestrates), Notary consumes the kernel's delta feed, and `workspace_topology.toml` plus the beads work graph are both checked acyclic in CI; `fa-reference` and `fa-verify` have registered allowlists (`fa-reference` depends on no engine crate and on no substrate crate — "no engine dependency" and "no foundation dependency" are both true for it, which is why it carries its own arithmetic); no crate is created before its first real slice (no empty prototype crates); every crate root is `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`; posture closures (`fa`, `fa-cli`, `fad`+`fa-mcp`) contain no test-only or packaging crates; `fa-mcp` is the only crate allowed to depend on `fastmcp-rust`.

### 18.2 Registries (`registries/`, validated by `tools/registry-check` at G0)

`constitution.toml` (claim classes, constraints FA-CON-01…14, the six bets), `invariants.toml` (FA-INV spine), `evidence.toml`, `slo.toml`, `checker_index.toml`, `proof_lanes.toml`, `threat_model.toml`, `workspace_topology.toml`, `foundation_dependencies.toml` (Appendix H as data: each substrate capability, status, interim posture, tracking bead), `symbols.toml`, `object_schemas/*.toml` (Appendix A as data, with the construction order and digest recipes), `units.toml` (unit versions and scale-conversion laws), `rounding_laws.toml`, `allocation_laws.toml`, `fx_laws.toml`, `lot_laws.toml`, `conversion_laws.toml`, `tracing_laws.toml`, `entry_classes.toml`, `supersession_kinds.toml`, `period_states.toml`, `admissibility.toml`, `reason_codes.toml`, `relationship_bases.toml`, `evidence_policy.toml`, `intents.toml`, `procedures.toml`, `capability_caveats.toml` (with the closed `ops` list), `sod_policy.toml`, `verbs.toml` (with the MCP mapping and registered exclusions), `exit_codes.toml`, `refusal_catalog.toml` (one exit per refusal), `formats.toml` (one owner crate per reader/writer), `robot_schema/*.json`, `bundle_schema/*.toml`, `operation_costs.toml` (Appendix G), `claims_lint.toml`.

### 18.3 Built in-house versus consumed

**Built here:** the accounting semantics and contracts in §2.7, reusing suitable suite primitives such as exact rational arithmetic. **Explicitly not built:** the storage engine, MVCC/SSI, system time, valid-time contracts, branches, incremental views, indexes, capability compilation, the audit pipeline, replay manifests, RaptorQ, encryption, the lab runtime, networking, supervision, the MCP protocol machinery, graph algorithms. Accounting laws remain owned here and deterministic under the lab runtime; their primitive implementations can come from other owner-maintained Franken projects.

### 18.4 Dependency policy

The sources below describe the existing integration, not an exhaustive list of eligible projects. Under the owner's 2026-10-10 clarification, adopting another Franken project adds its exact pin, source allowlist and actual transitive wrapper paths in the same change. Existing wrapper restrictions cannot be used as an indirect veto of that adoption. FrankenSymPy's rational integration is specified in §6.1 and tracked by `fa-pri`.

`Cargo.toml` pins `asupersync = { git, rev = "78b64636…", version = "=0.5.0", default-features = false }` plus `[patch.crates-io] asupersync = { git, rev = "78b64636…" }` so `fastmcp-*` (which pins the registry `=0.5.0`) and `fgdb` share one `Cx` (§0.4 decision 9); `frankengraphdb` crates by git `rev`; and `fnx-classes`/`fnx-algorithms = { git, rev = "9d710b1c…" }` at the revision `fgdb-prism` pins to avoid duplicate graph types. `fsym-bigint`, `fsym-rational` and `fsym-budget` use one exact FrankenSymPy revision. `deny.toml` admits those four git sources with `unknown-git = "deny"` and `required-git-spec = "rev"`; `fastmcp-rust = "=0.12.0"` remains confined to `fa-mcp`. `franken-stack.lock` records the revisions; each pin change updates its lock entries and is verified against the other foundations.

| Dependency | Source | Pin | Features | Closures |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `asupersync` | git `Dicklesworthstone/asupersync` | rev `78b64636e99fea4ea2d868096576021dd3b8e519` (= 0.5.0) | `default-features = false`; `tls` in `fad`/`fa-cli`; `test-internals` in dev-dependencies | all |
| `[patch.crates-io] asupersync` | same git rev | — | — | makes fastmcp's registry `=0.5.0` resolve to the same crate |
| `fgdb`, `fgdb-types`, `fgdb-bigint`, `fgdb-claim`, `fgdb-evidence`, `fgdb-crypto` | git `Dicklesworthstone/frankengraphdb` | rev pinned in `franken-stack.lock` (`b323bd17` at plan time) | default | `fa`, `fa-cli`, `fad`, `fa-mcp` (never `fa-reference`, `fa-verify`) |
| `fnx-classes`, `fnx-algorithms` | git `Dicklesworthstone/franken_networkx` | rev `9d710b1c33e99412c94de7fa4de2f7ce4954110f` (the rev `fgdb-prism` pins) | `default-features = false` | Already transitive in the `fgdb` posture closures through `fgdb-prism`; direct `fa-lantern` consumption is planned |
| `fastmcp-rust` | crates.io | `=0.12.0` | default | `fa-mcp` only |

`cargo metadata` in CI proves exactly one `asupersync`, one `fnx-classes`, and one `fgdb-types` package id per posture closure; a second copy fails the gate. `deny.toml` bans tokio, async-std, smol, rusqlite, sqlx, chrono, time, rust_decimal, bigdecimal, num-bigint, arrow, polars, petgraph, hyper, axum, reqwest, tantivy, and serde/ring except through exact wrappers — `[bans] deny = [{ name = "serde", wrappers = ["fastmcp-core", "fastmcp-server", "fastmcp-transport", "fastmcp-protocol", "fastmcp", <the asupersync wrappers the substrate already lists>] }, { name = "ring", wrappers = [...] }]` — and because `cargo deny` bans are workspace-global, posture confinement is enforced operationally by `scripts/check-forbidden-deps.sh` running `cargo tree -e normal -p fa -p fa-cli --invert serde` (and `ring`) and failing on any hit: that is what "confined to the `fa-mcp` posture closure" means. Test-only dependencies are a separate, registered allowlist (`[dev-dependencies]` only; `fsci-stats`; the external `hledger`, `ledger`, and `bean-check` binaries invoked by the oracle harness) that `cargo tree -e normal` proves absent from every posture closure; nothing enters it without a registry row. `franken-stack.lock` records each foundation's git commit and checksum. `scripts/check-forbidden-deps.sh` and `cargo deny` run in `scripts/check.sh`.

## 19. Workstreams and Convergence Gates

No MVP and no substitute architectures. Work is sequenced by dependency and converges through constitutional gate G0 plus four integration gates; the substrate's planned capabilities are consumed when they land (Appendix H), and the interim postures are themselves gated.

**Gate G0 — Constitutional Contracts (before any engine code):** every registry in §18.2 materialized with its schema and first rows; the FA-INV spine of Appendix F with clause activation predicates, checker entrypoints, and `waiver = "forbidden"`; the threat model; the admissibility matrix with its exhaustive generated test; the unit, law, class, intent, procedure, caveat, verb, exit-code, and refusal catalogs frozen at major 1; the canonical object schemas of Appendix A with `BodyDigest` recipes and golden vectors; the bundle and certificate schemas marked `externally_verifiable`; the robot-mode envelope and schema fixtures; the operation-cost registry (Appendix G) with the derivability law; `foundation_dependencies.toml` with every row's status checked against the substrate on the G0 date; `tools/registry-check` green; `scripts/check.sh` with the verdict contract; README/AGENTS synchronization checks. No workstream may invent a missing semantic choice locally.

| WS | Name | Contents | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|
| W1 | Bedrock | `fa-types`, `fa-money` (exact mul/div, laws, allocation, FX with Lean lanes), `fa-time` (calendars, periods, selectors, exhaustive tests), `fa-registry`, `fa-model` symbols, `fa-sim-harness` (lab runtime, crash points, oracles scaffold — before the first kernel line), `fa-reference` (the full semantics oracle with its own arithmetic), `fa-demo`/`fa-gen` (synthetic books) | G0 |
| W2 | Folio on the substrate | `fa-model` schema install as journaled schema effects; `fa-journal` posting pipeline with the balance law, leaf-only, unit policy, dimensions, idempotency, batches; projections; `fa-registers` maintainer over `delta_since` with checkpoints and verify-by-fold; two-phase entries and the expiry sweep; interim balance limits; subledger constraints; application and matching; the Genesis slice | W1 |
| W3 | Almanac + Palimpsest | Period state machine as control entries; admissibility at commit with point-read witnesses; selectors; close runs as DAGs with dry-run on drafts; auto-reverse and recurring schedules; entry classes; supersession lineage and netting; restatement cases with dual control and diff certificates; erasure and holds; migration | W2 |
| W4 | Docket + Notary | Documents (inline/external, public commitments, chunk trees), import receipts, attestations and balance assertions, bank statement parsers and bank lines, erasable fields; the journal accumulator maintained **in commit order off the write path** (§13.2) with leaf envelopes and the scope index; checkpoints and witnesses; certificates on every read; bundle format and production profiles; `fa-verify` with its own BLAKE3, Ed25519, and golden proofs | W2 |
| W5 | Teller | `fa-verbs` registry and parity checker; `fa` facade; `fa-cli` with robot mode, exit codes, refusal catalog, intent inference, mega-commands, doctor; `fad` daemon with writer lease and local RPC; `fa-mcp` over fastmcp with clusters, resources, macros, mistake detectors; Polish Bar regression tests; agent simulations of §15.6 | W2–W4 (early slices from W2) |
| W6 | Canon | Language, type checker, IR, interim evaluator under stripped `Cx`; intent catalog bound to rule families; dry-run plans and `confirm_plan`; golden corpora; backtesting; activation with dual control; VM adapter when R-VM lands | W2, W3 |
| W7 | Statements + Concordat | Statement definitions and the report engine with certificates; comparatives and restatement views; multi-book; intercompany constraint; consolidation runs on drafts/branches; revaluation and translation rules; regulatory schedules; XBRL export | W3, W4, W6 |
| W8 | Lantern | Procedure registry; JET, digit, duplicate, sequence, cutoff, aging, reconciliation, sampling; graph procedures over the money-flow projection with `fnx`; tracing laws; solvency and lookback; findings and workpapers; evidence rows and calibration; precision/recall on `fa-gen` scenarios | W2, W4, W7 |
| W9 | Interop + conformance | `fa-formats` readers/writers; ADS and SAF-T with proofs; Beancount/hledger differential oracle; TigerBeetle semantic corpus; XBRL validation; fuzz corpora | W2, W4, W7 |
| W10 | Verification + release | `fa-sim` scenario campaigns and crash matrices accrued per workstream over the W1 harness; oracles; metamorphic suites; TLA+ models; gauntlet; benchmark manifests and baselines; papers | all (the harness itself is W1) |
| W11 | Post-1.0 | TigerBeetle-compatible transfer API profile; FrankenAccounting over `fgdbd` (networked multi-tenant posture); `fa-frames` packaging boundary; substrate-branch materialization of drafts; Ripple-backed registers; storage-level MMR cross-anchoring; macOS/Windows filesystem profiles as the substrate ships them | 1.0 |

**The Genesis slice.** The first integration draws, by design, on W1, W2, and the following named *first slices* needed before their behavior is enabled — the period state machine and admissibility matrix from W3, the certificate wrapper and `fa-verify` of a trivial bundle from W4, the robot envelope and exit codes from W5, caveat evaluation from `fa-policy`, and real PostEntry, IntentHeader and Plan types from `fa-intents` — each registered as a clause-level first slice with its checker live before the behavior it enables (FA-INV-01/02/03.core/04/05.minimum/06/07.core/09/10.core/11.core/14.leaf-and-consistency/15.core/16). It proves, end to end and under the lab: create a book → install the chart and calendar as journaled schema effects → post a balanced two-leg entry via `PostEntry` through admit/resolve/laws/plan/commit → the derived legs exist and equal the payload → the register maintainer advances and `balance = fold` → crash at every barrier (between plan and commit, commit and ack, delta consumption and watermark) and recover → `fa tb --json` returns the certified trial balance → `fa verify registers` is green → an unbalanced proposal is refused with exit `3` and the per-unit imbalance → a post into a `Closed` period is refused with exit `7` and the matrix cell → `fa replay` reproduces the trial balance bytes. No stub register, no post-filter capability, no float, no wall clock.

W2 takes the real first fa-intents slice needed by Genesis before Folio's first slice. W6 later extends that same Laws-layer abstraction for Canon. This is a schedule clarification, not a crate-cycle remedy or no-op evaluator; rule-driven intents remain unsupported until their real Canon slice and law checkers exist.

**Gate G1 — "The Journal Lives":** Genesis slice green under lab and live; the Genesis clause set (FA-INV-01, 02, 03.core, 04, 05.minimum, 06, 07.core, 09, 10.core, 11.core, 14.leaf-and-consistency, 15.core, 16) live with checkers and the remaining clauses registered dormant with their activating workstream named; registers verified by fold on every fixture; idempotency and batches under crash; the reference differential over model-generated histories; the robot-mode envelope and `fa triage`, `fa post`, `fa tb`, `fa explain balance`, `fa doctor` at the Polish Bar; `fa-verify` verifies a produced bundle of the Genesis book.

**Gate G2 — "Time and Truth":** period state machine, admissibility matrix, and witness-resolved post-vs-close races proven in both orders; selectors including `AS ORIGINALLY REPORTED` and `AS REPORTED IN`; supersession netting and restatement cases with diff certificates; two-phase entries with expiry sweeps under lab; documents, custody, attestations, balance assertions; accumulator continuity and witnessed checkpoints; FA-INV-03/07/08/10/14 live; the Beancount/hledger differential green on all fixtures; Canon rules with golden corpora and backtests; dry-run plans with `confirm_plan`.

**Gate G3 — "Agents and Auditors":** the full verb registry across CLI/daemon/MCP with the parity gate green; fresh-context simulated agents complete every §15.6 workflow with zero `useless_error` outcomes in the intent corpus; SoD and caveats enforced at commit (FA-INV-10 full); statements and consolidation certified; Lantern's procedures with evidence rows and measured precision/recall; ADS/SAF-T exports with proofs; `fa demo init` scenarios; the gauntlet's surface-parity and ergonomics scorecards published.

**Gate G4 — "Published":** all activated §17 manifests green and public; conformance matrices (ADS, SAF-T, XBRL, Beancount/hledger, TigerBeetle corpus) published with documented divergences; every FA-INV clause live or its capability absent from the manifest; the papers match the proven scope; `fa-verify` released as a static binary with golden proofs; 1.0.

## 20. Risks and Mitigations

| Risk | Reality check | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| The substrate is unreleased and several load-bearing surfaces are planned (valid time, branches, Ripple views, escrow, MMR, UDF VM) | True; it is also being completed in parallel by a swarm, and the owner's call is to build on it | Appendix H names every dependency, its status, its interim posture, and its bead; the reference oracle and W1 do not depend on the substrate *engine* at all (`fa-reference` depends on no foundation crate either and carries its own arithmetic); interim postures are permanent-correct designs (intent-log drafts, journal-level accumulator, `delta_since` maintainer) rather than throwaways; a substrate regression is caught by the pinned `rev` and `franken-stack.lock` |
| Hot-account contention before escrow lands | Real on cash and clearing accounts | Registers off the posting path; the `LimitCell` reservation cell only for limited accounts (§5.5); the abort-rate gate is reported in both postures |
| Period law and SoD at commit raise abort rates for long-running agent sessions | They will, by design | Dry-run plans are cheap; `PlanInvalidated` carries a fresh plan; agents work on drafts and promote in one batch |
| Exact arithmetic without a decimal crate | `CanonicalDecimal` lacks mul/div; building it is real work | `fa-money` is W1 with Lean lanes and exhaustive vectors; `fgdb-bigint` provides the wide intermediates; nothing else in the system may do arithmetic on amounts |
| Calendar and date arithmetic bugs | The classic source of "wrong period" defects | Brute-force reference over 1900–2100; golden vectors for every calendar kind including 53-week years; no external crate to disagree with |
| Legal and regulatory claims overreach | A plan that says "FRE 902(14)-compliant" is a liability | §13.4's rule: state technical predicates, never admissibility or compliance; counsel maps |
| Forensic detectors produce false accusations | Statistics are not invariants | Claim typing (§11.9); findings are observations with evidence; thresholds are decision cards; precision/recall measured on labeled corpora and published |
| Agent surface drift between CLI, daemon, and MCP | Three surfaces will diverge without a gate | `verbs.toml` is the single source; the parity checker fails CI; the Polish Bar regression tests pin every applied ergonomics change |
| fastmcp_rust pins a different nightly and carries serde/ring | Confined to one posture closure | `deny.toml` per-crate allowlist; `fa-mcp` built on this repo's pin with the drift tracked as a bead; the daemon's local RPC does not depend on fastmcp |
| Scope creep toward an ERP | Every subledger invites workflow | Anti-goals in §0.3; subledgers are modeled as books and control constraints, never as workflow |
| Erasure obligations versus immutability | A real legal tension | Typed tombstones, attributable grade downgrade, legal holds, amounts never in scope (§8.5) |
| Performance versus a single-core batched engine | FrankenAccounting will not match TigerBeetle's transfer throughput, and should not claim to | The §17 gates are derived from the substrate's barrier chain; the product claim is a system of record with proofs, not a payments core; the compat profile states its envelope |
| Restatement semantics misread by practitioners | "Big R / little r" and retrospective application have edge cases | Cases are explicit objects with kinds and disclosure documents; the diff certificate makes the effect visible; the matrix is exhaustive and generated |
| The plan is too long to act on | A fair worry | §19 decomposes into beads with the dependency graph intact; every section names its registry rows and gate |
| Single-writer embedded handle | All throughput is one process holding one `&mut Database`; multi-agent concurrency is interleaved `WriteTxn`s whose conflicts are first-committer-wins aborts | The honest number is the abort rate under the §17 fixtures, not a contention gate; drafts promote as batches; group commit is a substrate row (R-GC) |
| Substrate parser instability | Several statement parsers race upstream during consolidation | `fa-query` targets `Database::query` only and `fa-conformance` pins the exact native statement corpus it may emit |
| Recovery fences | `RecoveryRequired`/`ensure_readable` refusals from the substrate | Map to `exit 5` with `fa doctor` guidance; `fad` never auto-repairs; the operator runs the substrate's recovery procedure |
| Retired delta cursor forces a full register rebuild | Real whenever checkpoint cadence lags retention | `checkpoint_cadence ≤ retention_floor` is an `slo.toml` row; the 10M-leg rebuild gate (§17) bounds the cost |
| Substrate HLC absent | `AS KNOWN AT <instant>` resolves through journaled ticks only | §7.1a ticks are a permanent corroboration design; the upstream HLC bead is tracked (R-HLC) |

### 20.1 Reality check and bridge plan — 2026-10-23

**Assessment: a substantial accounting implementation, with a useful demonstrated
slice, but the six-bet system and G0–G4 acceptance are not yet established.**
This section records an audit and remaining work; it neither changes the target
contracts above nor closes implementation beads. The generated workflow scoreboard
is a sample of commands, not a percentage of the complete product delivered.

#### Current-cut refresh (2026-10-26, StormyJay)

**Assessment: the product is broad and mostly honest about itself. Two foundations are not yet real: its verdicts and its scale.** Breadth is outrunning both. The review cut is origin/main `8a17bb7bd`.

**How the check was made:**
- AGENTS.md and README.md were read in full, plus §19 and this section.
- Five independent read-only subsystem audits re-scored V01–V37.
- Fresh debug `fa` and `fa-verify` were built from that commit (rch, standalone clone) and run end to end on a new book.
- The checker read the `refs/notes/fa-verdict` and `refs/notes/fa-tier0` notes, the recent full-test logs and the bead graph (442 issues: 139 open, 50 in progress, 253 closed).
- Bead graph: epic `fa-0hs4` and its 17 children. Where an existing owner covers a gap, the finding is a comment on that owner instead.

**Executed (synthetic inputs):**
- All 21 `### Try it today` commands exit 0, and `fa replay` reproduces the certified trial balance.
- The Genesis negatives hold: unbalanced → exit 3 FA-REF-001; post into Closed → exit 7 FA-REF-010; a float amount → exit 2; no evidence → exit 3 FA-REF-080.
- `fa-verify` accepts the prefix, account bundle and certified-TB report; a one-byte bundle tamper exits 8.
- Self-approval is refused at commit (exit 7 FA-REF-008).
- `fa demo init --scenario worldcom` and the JET, Benford and related-party procedures run.

**Product findings:**
- Single posts: 118 ms → 439 ms across 300 posts (about 1.1 ms more per entry already in the book, debug build).
- `fa verify book` takes 15 s at 324 entries and **exits 8 on a healthy book** whenever a period holds entries but was never materialized. An absent register cache is reported as corruption (`fa-registers` lib.rs:1040); this is `fa-0hs4.5`.
- Dual control on the owner CLI is satisfied by typing a second `--as` name.

**Verdict findings:**
- **No genuine full verdict exists.** Every `fa-verdict` PASS note records only 6–8 selftest/shellcheck steps.
- **Some notes carry copied step records.** On 90fbbcc4c (the README badge) the steps carry the parent's tree. Three notes for three trees share one `started_utc`, 10–11 h before those commits were authored.
- **Seven closures cite these notes.**
- **The last complete workspace run was 24c64e79** (3407 passed / 15 failed). Main has since taken 404 commits. The latest attempt was killed at the 7,200 s cap (1671 passed / 21 failed).
- **Tier 0 is not enforced.** Only 20 of the last 150 first-parent main commits carry a Tier-0 note, and no hooks are installed.

#### Source, execution, and claim boundaries

#### Bridge sequence and success criteria

1. **Recover one trustworthy baseline (W10/G0–G1).** fa-abhg supplies admitted
   canonical worker capacity; fa-ze0y proves repaired MCP discovery and daemon
   fixtures at one committed candidate. Run Tier 0, the complete test inventory,
   Clippy and `scripts/check.sh`, then verify and publish its exact-tree manifest.
   Keep prior red runs and expected-failure controls. First repair the verified
   resume trust defects through fa-hlnd.1, preserving valid partial reuse and
   publishing admitted/refused controls. fa-1qk0/fa-j1fc/fa-8jcr must
   make that path govern main and closure; fa-ubl0 transcribes every §19 criterion
   without equating a CLI sample with lab, oracle, proof or measurement evidence.
2. **Protect admitted accounting (W2–W3/G1–G2).** Finish capacity/overflow,
   aggregate basis, idempotent outcomes, principal/approval bindings and timing
   laws before adding throughput. Tests must cover both prepared commit orders,
   intervening metadata/ticks/AccessEvents, crash before and after publication,
   same-key changed-content refusal, exact uncertain-ack retry and unchanged
   accounting state on refusal. Include batch, draft/case, recurring effects and
   migration Finalize; the latest two source additions create no exemption.
3. **Complete the operator workflows (W3–W8/G2–G3).** Extend the existing
   CloseRun/case, Canon, authority, report and Concordat owners in the table,
   retaining code and its unit/real-binary tests together. Promote a checklist
   row only after its real CLI/fad/MCP consumer works under the intended profile.
   No duplicate reporting engine, mutable register truth, substitute storage or
   blanket global lock is introduced. Unit evolution, privacy and foundation
   switch-overs retain their explicit version/admission rules.
4. **Prove the evidence joins (W4/W8–W10/G2–G4).** Complete offline statement
   and scenario closure, independent scope derivation, actual external oracles,
   labeled forensic calibration, generated-history differentials, crash matrix,
   real Lean/TLC, fuzzing and measured workloads. Companion acceptance campaigns
   independently test the implementation; ordinary unit and e2e tests still ship
   in each implementation bead. Log source/tree, seed, coordinate, capability
   commitment, work counters, artifact hashes and verdict; never log secrets.
5. **Deliver the inspected artifact (G4).** Qualify the source-present installer
   checks and bind source installation to the actual Cargo target outputs;
   validate reproducibility on distinct builders and
   verify each target's actual linkage rather than calling std-only “static.”
   The current reproducibility script does two same-host `rustc` builds, declares
   but never invokes a perturbation control, and has a deletion trap; it was not
   executed in this session. Final delivery uses DSR and a new destination for
   installation/air-gap tests, binds all results to the exact artifacts, and
   publishes only the four §16.10 reports' supported predicates. No live release
   is authorized merely by creating these planning beads.

Each implementation owner retains its normal tests, negative controls and full
gate obligation. A release-wide acceptance task cannot close its implementation
dependencies, and a document change cannot substitute for them. Most product
gaps already have detailed open beads; the new coverage gap is the **final
acceptance and delivery join** after previously closed release engineering, plus
the reproduced installer bug. Completing old issue titles alone would miss it.
