## Appendix A — Canonical Object Schemas (normative contract)

All durable FrankenAccounting objects are properties and payloads of substrate elements and therefore inherit the substrate's Appendix A encoding laws: declared format major/minor; little-endian fixed integers; canonical unsigned LEB128 only where a field says varint; length-before-allocation; checked arithmetic; per-kind maximum sizes; unknown additive-minor fields length-delimited and skipped only under a registered `canonical_default_law_id`; unknown required flags fail closed. Every `BodyDigest<T>` row declares `{domain_separator, schema_major, included_field_tags, excluded_field_tags}` and uses the substrate's recipe `BLAKE3(domain_separator ‖ canonical(schema_major, T excluding exactly the one BodyDigest field))`. The pseudo-schemas below are checked renderings of `registries/object_schemas/*.toml`; a field absent from the registry is unencodable.

`EntryProposal` and the committed `Entry` use the explicit recipes below. Their registry rows enumerate exact exclusions and component encodings; the generic whole-body BodyDigest recipe does not override them. Producer and verifier use the same normative preimage per domain separator.

Any changed positional schema or existing field type requires its explicit breaking-major/version transition and matching encoder, decoder, registry and independent vectors before activation. These renderings specify the selected target contract; they do not silently redefine bytes already implemented as v1. Until the matching checker and representation exist, affected behavior remains unsupported.

```text
Amount            { unit_id: u32, mantissa: i128 }                       // scale from units.toml; no exponent field
Rate              { numerator: BigIntCanonical, denominator: BigIntCanonical }   // exact rational; denominator > 0
DimensionSet      { entries: sorted [(dimension_id: u32, value_ref: VId)] }       // canonical sort by dimension_id
EntryRef          { vid: VId128, entry_digest: [u8;32] }
ProposalRef       { vid: VId128, proposal_digest: [u8;32] }
EntryTarget       = Committed { entry_ref: EntryRef }
                  | Proposed { proposal_ref: ProposalRef }  // earlier member of the same ordered manifest only
EffectManifest    { book_id, target_coordinate, execution_order: [ProposalRef],
                    authorization_refs: sorted [TypedRef],
                    effects_digest: BLAKE3("fa:effects:v1" ‖ canonical(all preceding fields)) }
IntentOutcome     { book_id, principal_scope, idempotency_key: Bytes(1..=64), intent_oid: Oid,
                    result: Committed { entries: ordered [EntryRef] } | Refused { canonical_refusal: Bytes }, body_digest }
ApprovalRef       { approval_vid: VId128, approved_digest: [u8;32] }

Entry (schema fa:entry:v1)                                                // §4.2; explicit envelope-digest recipe below, not the generic whole-body recipe
  { schema_major: u16 = 1, schema_minor: u16,
    book_id: Id128, branch_id: Id128,
    class: EntryClass (u16 tag from entry_classes.toml),
    effective_date: Option<DateDay>,         // Some for Period scope; None for Book controls; DateDay = i32 days since 1970-01-01, proleptic Gregorian
    document_date: Option<DateDay>,
    description: Erasable<String (≤ 1024 bytes UTF-8 NFC)>,
    effective_period: Option<PeriodRef>,     // discriminator when the date lies in a range shared with a special period (§7.3)
    legs: List<LegPayload> (registered arity ≤ max_legs_per_entry; posting arity ≥ 2 except registered SingleSided statistical legs; Resolution is disposition-sensitive under §5.4; control classes and Resolution(Voids|Expires): exactly 0),
    control: Option<ControlPayload>,         // present iff legs is empty: control classes or Resolution(Voids|Expires)
    unit_totals: sorted Map<unit_id, { debits: i128, credits: i128 }>,
    base_totals: Option<{ unit_id, debits: i128, credits: i128, fx_law_oid: Oid, rate_doc_oids: sorted [Oid], residual: Option<{ leg_ordinal: u16, amount: Amount }> }>,   // residual as §5.2: absorbed by one designated leg
    idempotency_key: Bytes (1..=64),
    origin: EntryOrigin,
    principal_ref: VId128, session_ref: Option<VId128>,
    proposal_digest: BLAKE3("fa:entry-proposal:v1" ‖ canonical(every field except plan_certificate_oid, approvals, proposal_digest, entry_digest)),
    plan_certificate_oid: Option<Oid>,       // ENVELOPE field: the dry-run certificate is computed over a plan that already contains proposal_digest, so it can only bind here (acyclic); None for rule-, sweep-, control-, and migration-origin entries
    approvals: [ApprovalRef],                // each binds proposal_digest; the transcript is part of the envelope
    evidence: sorted [{ document_oid: Oid (strong), doc_commit: [u8;32], role: EvidenceRole(u8) }],
    supersession: [{ target: EntryRef, kind: SupersessionKind(u8), reason_code: u16 }],
    pending: Option<{ timeout_ns: Option<u64>, resolution_of: Option<EntryRef>, disposition: Option<Disposition> }>,
    external_ids: sorted [{ system: String(≤64), id: Erasable<String(≤256)> }],
    tags: sorted Map<String(≤64), Erasable<TagValue>> (≤ 32 entries, ≤ 4 KiB),   // TagValue = Null | Bool | Int(i64) | Text(≤256) | Bytes(≤1024): the storable scalar subset under FrankenAccounting's own codec, so the durable format never tracks the substrate's scalar encoding
    entry_digest: BLAKE3("fa:entry:v1" ‖ proposal_digest ‖ canonical(plan_certificate_oid) ‖ canonical(approvals)) }   // the committed envelope

ControlPayload (closed union; legs are empty; explicit u8 tags pinned by the declared schema version's object_schemas registry, never inferred from rendering order)
  = PeriodTransition   { period_ref, from: u8, to: u8, reason_code: u16, case_ref: Option<VId128> }
  // Preserve all existing variant discriminants; admit ReservationRelease only after assigning a fresh unused tag in the declared version and pinning codec fixtures.
  | SchemaChange       { kind: u16, canonical_bytes: Bytes }              // chart, dimension, unit-version, calendar, symbol table
  | PolicyActivation   { policy_oid: Oid, effective: [DateDay, DateDay) }  // rule versions, laws, evidence policy, SoD policy
  | LifecycleTransition { object_ref: VId128, from: u8, to: u8, proof_refs: [TypedRef], reason_code: u16 }
  | TimeTick           { observed_utc_ns: u64, source: u8, monotonic_ok: bool }
  | SweepCursor        { sweep_kind: u8, last_pending_vid: VId128, tick_ref: VId128 }
  | ScheduleCancelled  { schedule_ref: VId128, cause_ref: VId128 }
  | CapabilityEvent    { commitment: [u8;32], kind: Issue | Revoke | Attenuate, caveats_digest }
  | HoldEvent          { scope_digest, authority_oid: Oid, kind: Place | Release }
  | ErasureCut         { subject_ref: VId128, field_digests: sorted [[u8;32]] }
  | MigrationControl   { run_ref: VId128, kind: Open | Finalize | Abandon, tie_out_digest }
  | ReservationRelease {}                              // Resolution(Voids|Expires); target/disposition live in pending

Erasable<T>           { profile: ErasableProfileRef, context: { subject_ref: Option<VId128>, type_id: TypeId, field_role: FieldRole, key_epoch: KeyEpoch }, field_digest: [u8;32], nonce: [u8;24], sealed: Bytes }   // versioned profile defines canonical context encoding, randomized foundation AEAD, authenticated expected context, and the fresh salted field commitment; salt stays encrypted. The parent digest covers this full immutable wire (§8.5). This target shape is not an admission claim for the current codec; no guessing-security guarantee follows from the salt alone.

LegPayload (wire type, inline)
  { side: u8 (0 = Debit, 1 = Credit),
    account_ref: VId128,
    amount: Amount,
    base_amount: Option<Amount>,
    dimension_set: DimensionSet,
    memo: Option<Erasable<String(≤ 512)>>,
    settles: [{ target_leg: (EntryTarget, ordinal: u16), amount: Amount }] }

EntryOrigin (closed union)
  = Intent     { intent_oid: Oid, intent_kind: u16 }                                   // the plan certificate is an envelope field, never part of the proposal it is computed from
  | Rule       { rule_version_oid: Oid, event_ref: VId128, inputs_digest: [u8;32] }
  | Migration  { run_ref: VId128, source_system: String(≤64), source_id: Erasable<String(≤256)>, source_recorded_at: Option<i64 ns>,
                 source_entered_by: Option<Erasable<String>>, source_approved_by: Option<Erasable<String>>, batch_oid: Oid }
  | Control    { control_kind: u16, spec_body_digest: [u8;32] }
  | Sweep      { sweep_kind: u8, tick_evidence_oid: Oid }

Disposition = Posts { source_ordinals: [u16] }          // parallel to Entry.legs; original pending-leg ordinals; intent sugar stays in Appendix B
            | Voids | Expires { at_tick: u64 }

Account           { book_id, code: String(≤64), name, type: u8, normal_balance: u8, posting: u8,
                    unit_policy: Single(u32) | AnyCurrency | AnyUnit, control_of: Option<VId128>,
                    dimension_requirements: sorted [u32], balance_limit: Option<EscrowDomainRef>,
                    _valid_from, _valid_to }                               // valid time via contract or interim property
Period            { calendar_id, code: String(≤32), fiscal_year: i32, ordinal: u16, kind: u8,
                    start: DateDay, end: DateDay (half-open), state: u8 (versioned; every change is a control entry) }
Unit              { code: String(≤16), kind: u8 (+ iso4217 numeric for currencies), scale: u8 (≤ 18),
                    rounding_default: u16 (rounding_laws.toml), symbol, display_scale: u8 }
Register (derived native measure; functional folds registered under §5.3) { key: { book_id, branch_id, account_ref, unit_id, period_ref, dimension_subset_key: [u8;16], rollup: bool },
                    debits_posted: i128, credits_posted: i128, debits_pending: i128, credits_pending: i128,
                    entry_count: u64, watermark: CommitSeq, fold_digest: [u8;32] }
RegisterCheckpoint { definition_oid, commit_seq: CommitSeq, cells_root: [u8;32], cell_count: u64, body_digest }
Document          { oid: Oid (substrate ObjectId of canonical bytes), kind: u8, mime: String(≤128), size: u64,
                    storage: Inline { object_ref } | External { locator: String(≤2048), fetch_proof: { digest, import_receipt_oid } },
                    parsed: Option<{ parser_id: u16, parser_version: u32, projection_oid: Oid }>,
                    redaction: Option<RedactionContractRef>, retrieved_by: VId128, custody: ImportReceiptRef }
ImportReceipt     { source: Upload{principal} | Connector{name, version} | Email{message_id} | Scan{device},
                    digest_at_receipt: [u8;32], transformations: [{ kind: u16, version: u32, output_digest }], body_digest }
Attestation       { claim: AttestationClaim, subject_refs: sorted [TypedRef], principal_ref, evidence_refs: sorted [Oid],
                    signed_digest: [u8;32] = BLAKE3("fa:attestation:v1" ‖ canonical(claim, subject_refs, principal_ref, evidence_refs)),   // excludes signature and itself
                    signature: { scheme: u16 (Ed25519), key_id, bytes } }
AttestationClaim  = Prepared | Reviewed { against: [Oid] } | Reconciled { instrument_ref, through: DateDay }
                  | RegisterEquals { cell_key, amount: Amount, as_of_effective: DateDay, evaluated_known_at: CommitSeq, fold_digest: [u8;32] }   // immutable; drift is a monitor finding (§9.4)
                  | Confirmed { by_party: VId128, balance: Amount, as_of: DateDay } | Examined { bundle_oid } | Custom { kind: u16, body_digest }
Approval          { capability_ref, principal_ref, approved_proposal_digest: [u8;32], effects_digest: [u8;32], book_id, target_coordinate, security_policy_epoch: u64, role: u16, body_digest }   // binds the proposal, never the committed envelope; recorded sequence = created_at
RestatementCase   (lifecycle object) body { kind: u8, periods_affected: sorted [PeriodRef], determination: Option<{ day: DateDay, authority_oid }>, narrative_oid: Option<Oid>, proposal_digest }
                    projection { status: u8, rehearsal_certificate_oid: Option<Oid>, disclosure_oid: Option<Oid> }   // approvals are Approval objects binding proposal_digest; transitions are LifecycleTransition control entries
CloseRun          { book_id, period_ref, mode: DryRun | Execute, draft_ref: Option<VId128>, tasks: [CloseTaskRef],
                    rehearsal_certificate_oid: Option<Oid>, status: u8 }
DraftSet          { owner_principal, name, planned_intents: [{ intent_oid, plan_certificate_oid, order: u32 }],
                    status: Open | Promoted { batch_entry_refs } | Discarded { reason } }
Finding           { procedure_id: u16, procedure_version: u32, parameters_digest, coordinate: Coordinate, severity: u8,
                    statistic: CanonicalPropertyValue, population: { count: u64, scope_digest }, cites: [TypedRef],
                    narrative_template_id: u16, status: u8, certificate_oid: Oid, body_digest }
Certificate       { closure: { book_root: [u8;32], branch_head: MarkerRef, known_at: SystemSelector,
                               resolved_sequence: CommitSeq, resolution_frontier: MarkerRef,
                               instant_resolution: Option<{ lower_tick: EntryRef,
                                 upper_bracket: Open | Closed { upper_tick: EntryRef }, precision_grade: TickBucket }>,
                               effective: EffectiveSelector,
                               subject: Definition{oid}|Procedure{id,version}|Plan{intent_oid}|Bundle{scope_digest}|Query{normalized_digest},
                               parameters_digest, registry_epochs: sorted Map<registry_id, epoch>, rule_versions: sorted [Oid],
                               laws: sorted [u16], inputs: sorted [Oid], capability_commitment: [u8;32], numeric_profile_oid },
                    output_digest: [u8;32], replay_command: String, completeness_grade: u8, body_digest }
AccumulatorState  { commit_seq: CommitSeq, leaf_count: u64, peaks: [[u8;32]], root: [u8;32], prev_state_oid: Option<Oid> }
LeafEnvelope      { kind: u8, book_id, branch_id, commit_seq: u64, within_commit_ordinal: u32, record_digest: [u8;32], class: u16, effective_day: Option<i32>, account_vids: sorted [VId128], unit_ids: sorted [u32] }
ScopeIndex (derived) { checkpoint_ref, tuples: sorted [(book_id, branch_id, account_vid, effective_day, leaf_index)], root: [u8;32] }
TransparencyCheckpoint { book_id, commit_seq, root, leaf_count, scope_index_root: [u8;32], substrate_chain_commitment: [u8;32], witness_policy_oid: Option<Oid>, time_evidence_oid,
                    body_digest = BLAKE3("fa:checkpoint:v1" ‖ canonical(all preceding fields)),   // excludes signatures
                    signatures: sorted [{ signer_key_id, scheme, bytes }] }
TrustAnchorSet    { signers: sorted [{ key_id, public_key, valid: [CommitSeq, CommitSeq) }], root_signature }
ProductionReceipt { bundle_digest: [u8;32], producer_principal, produced_at_seq, capability_commitment, time_evidence_oid, signature }
Bundle (.fab)     { manifest: { book_identity, coordinate, redaction_contract_oid: Option<Oid>, scope: BundleScope,
                               completeness_scope: SeqRangeComplete{from,to} | ScopeIndexComplete | FilterAttested, scope_label: u8, checkpoint_oid },   // deterministic body: no producer, no time
                    entries: [Entry], closures: [{ entry_digest, documents: [Document | DocumentStub{doc_commit, kind, length, disclosed_chunks}], events, rules, approvals }],
                    leaf_envelopes: [LeafEnvelope], inclusion_proofs: [{ leaf_index: u64, path: [[u8;32]] }], scope_proof: Option<{ tuples, boundary_neighbors, paths }>, checkpoint: TransparencyCheckpoint,
                    registers: [{ cell, value, fold_legs: [(EntryRef, ordinal)] }], statements: [{ rows, certificate }],
                    findings: [Finding], manifest_md: String, bundle_digest: BodyDigest<Bundle> }   // a ProductionReceipt travels beside the bundle, never inside it
SupplementalEvidence { entry_ref, documents: sorted [{ document_oid, doc_commit, role }], attached_by: VId128, body_digest }   // recorded sequence = created_at
UsageCell (interim, §14.2) { root_issuance_commitment: [u8;32], day: DateDay, unit_id, gross_debits: Decimal, generation: Int }
Capability (metadata only) { token_commitment: [u8;32], issuer_principal, holder_principal, caveats: sorted [Caveat], issued_seq, expires_at, revoked: Option<{ seq, reason }> }
StatementIssuance { definition_oid, period_ref, certificate_oid, issued_to: u8, document_oid: Option<Oid>, body_digest }
TimeEvidence      { observed_utc_ns: u64, source: DaemonClock | AuthorityDocument{oid} | SubstrateMarker, monotonic_ok: bool, body_digest }
MigrationRun      { source_system, mode: OpeningOnly | PeriodBalances{periods} | FullDetail, trial_balance_oid, opening_date: DateDay, chunks: [ChunkRef], attestation_oid: Option<Oid>, status: u8 }
Engagement        { firm_principal, kind: u8, scope_periods: [PeriodRef], materiality: { overall: Amount, performance: Amount, clearly_trivial: Amount }, report_release_day: Option<DateDay>, documentation_completion_day: Option<DateDay>, capability_commitment, workpaper_index_oid }
ProposedAdjustment { engagement_ref, draft_entry_payload: Bytes, status: Proposed | Booked{entry_ref} | Passed{reason_code, principal_ref}, body_digest }
ProductionProfile { matter_id, bates_prefix: String(≤16), next_bates: u64, designations: sorted Map<Oid, u8>, protective_order_oid: Option<Oid>, load_file_format: u8 }
LimitCell (interim, §5.5) { account_ref, reserved_debits: Decimal, reserved_credits: Decimal, generation: Int }
AccessEvent       { capability_commitment, principal_ref, snapshot_seq, query_digest, objects_touched_digest, kind: u8 }
```

Reference semantics: `TypedRef` is the substrate's exact tagged reference union generated per containing schema; **Reference law for this appendix:** every `*_oid`, `*_ref`, and `*_vid` field is a retaining strong reference unless its row is marked `weak` (comparison only) or `locator` (nonretaining), so FA-INV-05's retention is a generated check rather than a convention; `document_oid` in evidence lists is strong and is accompanied by the public `doc_commit`; `locator` strings are weak; erasure converts a strong reference to a strong tombstone reference whose target records the erased field digests; `certificate_oid` on a `Finding` is strong and points to an object constructed earlier (acyclic by the construction order below). Storage form: every schema above is encoded as a canonical `Bytes` payload on its vertex with scalar mirrors for predicates (§4.2 storage form), because the substrate stores scalar properties only; `DateDay` is mirrored as `Int` days, amounts as `Decimal` when representable and otherwise as `Bytes`. **Construction order (normative, acyclic):** `EntryProposal`s (earlier ProposalRefs only) → `proposal_digest`s → `EffectManifest` → `effects_digest` → dry-run `Certificate` (over the plan, which contains the proposal and effects digests) → `Approval`s (over `proposal_digest`, `effects_digest`, coordinate, policy epoch) → `Entry` envelope (proposal + plan_certificate_oid + ordered approvals) → `entry_digest` → `IntentOutcome` (binding the committed ordered EntryRefs) → `LeafEnvelope` → `AccumulatorState`/`ScopeIndex` → `TransparencyCheckpoint` (signed last) → report/procedure `Certificate`s over existing outputs → `Finding`s that cite them → `Bundle` body → `ProductionReceipt`. Lifecycle bodies hash before their first transition; transitions and projections never enter a body digest. A generated construction-DAG test rejects any schema row whose digest recipe could include a later object, including an Approved transition made a member of its own approved manifest. `Oid` is a substrate `ObjectId`; `VId128` is the substrate's 128-bit element identity; `MarkerRef`/`CommitSeq` are the substrate's. No FrankenAccounting object stores a float, a wall-clock instant it read itself, or a future object's identity.

`known_at` retains the requested selector; `resolved_sequence` is the concrete read/replay prefix, bounded by `resolution_frontier` on the named book/branch. For `Head` it is that frontier's sequence; for `AsOfSeq` it is the admitted requested sequence. `instant_resolution` is present exactly for `AsOfTime`; both tick references resolve to retained TimeEvidence controls, whose payloads carry their source grades. The lower tick is at or before the resolved sequence and its observed instant is at or before the requested instant. A closed upper bracket names the first later tick with an observed instant greater than the requested instant within the resolution frontier; otherwise it is explicitly Open. No current head is consulted to re-resolve the selector during replay. This rendering completes the existing retention obligation; its canonical registry encoding must land with its first codec.

`disclosed_chunks` is a bounded canonical list of `{chunk_index, bytes, proof}` or one canonical multiproof under a registered tagged law. Reuse §9.1's fixed 4 KiB chunks and `fa:doc:v1` commitment over kind, length and chunk_root. The grammar fixes tree shape, leaf/node domain separation, odd/final-partial-chunk handling, count/index derivation from committed length, canonical ordering, withheld chunks and sub-chunk disclosure_only, empty/whole-document cases, and rejection of duplicate/out-of-range indices. Apply Appendix A's size/allocation bounds before decoding. Complete this grammar at G0 or register the DocumentStub arm as absent until complete. Independent producer/fa-verify BLAKE3 vectors and decoder fuzzing validate the implementation; format completion itself proves neither implementation.

## Appendix B — Intent Catalog (normative fields)

Every intent carries the common header `{ book, branch?: draft|branch name, idempotency_key, dry_run: bool, confirm_plan?: CertificateRef, evidence?: [DocumentRef], description?: String, tags?: Map }`. Required capability and admissible classes are registry columns; field types use Appendix A.

`intent_oid` hashes the canonical accounting request: its verb, typed body, book and intended target, idempotency key, evidence, description, and tags. Transport controls `dry_run` and `confirm_plan` are excluded, so confirmation does not change the identity of the request rehearsed. Execution mode and scratch-book location do not silently change the intended target branch. A changed semantic field creates a changed intent identity. The proposal continues to bind every semantic Entry field specified in Appendix A, including provenance and supersession.

| Intent | Fields | Produces |
|---|---|---|
| `PostEntry` | `class`, `effective_date`, `legs: [{side, account: code|ref, amount: {unit, mantissa}|"1234.56 USD" (parsed exactly), base_amount?, dimensions?: {dim: value}, memo?, settles?}]`, `supersedes?`, `pending?` | 1 entry |
| `PostBatch` | `intents: [Intent]` (bounded), `atomic: true` | N entries, all or none |
| `RecordEvent` | `kind`, `occurred_on`, `party?`, `lines: [{quantity: {unit, mantissa}, unit_price?: Amount, item?, dims?}]`, `attributes: Map`, `documents: [DocumentRef]` | event + rule-derived entries |
| `RecordInvoice` / `RecordBill` | `party`, `invoice_date`, `due_date?`, `lines`, `tax_jurisdiction?`, `dims?`, `external_id?` | event + entries |
| `ApplyPayment` / `ApplyReceipt` | `instrument`, `amount`, `date`, `applications: [{open_item: EntryRef|external_id, amount}]`, `on_account?: bool`, `differences?: [{kind: Discount | ShortPay | Writeoff | BankCharge | RealizedFx, account?: code (defaulted by `application_policy`), amount}]` — every difference between cash and the open amount is an explicit reason-typed leg in the plan; a difference without a kind is a refusal naming the open amount; cross-currency applications route through the conversion account (§5.2) | entry with `settles` |
| `Reserve` / `PostReserve` / `VoidReserve` | `Reserve`: as `PostEntry` with `class: Pending`, `timeout?`; `PostReserve`: `pending: EntryRef`, `posted_legs: [LegPayload] | amount (two-leg sugar) | proportion: {law, rate}`; `VoidReserve`: `pending: EntryRef` | pending / resolution (§5.4) |
| `Accrue` / `Defer` | `effective_date`, `legs`, `auto_reverse_on?: DateDay` | adjusting (+ scheduled reversal) |
| `Reclass` | `from: [{leg: (EntryRef, ordinal)|account+dims, amount}]`, `to: [{account, dims, amount}]`, `reason_code` | reclassification |
| `Reverse` | `target: EntryRef` with admitted target-class netting law (§8.2), `effective_date?` (default: target's; may be the next open period), `reason_code` | reversal; unsupported target classes are refused |
| `Replace` | `target: EntryRef`, `replacement: PostEntry fields`, `reason_code` | reversal + replacement batch |
| `Allocate` | `source: {account, dims?, amount|AllOfPeriod}`, `law: AllocationLawId`, `targets: [{account, dims, weight: Rate|Amount}]`, `driver_document?` | allocation |
| `Revalue` / `Translate` | `period`, `law: FxLawId`, `rate_documents: [DocumentRef]`, `scope: accounts|entity` | revaluation entries |
| `Depreciate` / `Amortize` | `instrument|asset refs`, `period`, `schedule_rule` | adjusting entries |
| `MatchBank` / `ConfirmMatch` / `AssertBalance` | `instrument`, `statement: DocumentRef` / `matches: [MatchRef]` / `cell, amount, as_of_effective, evidence` | MATCHES edges / attestation |
| `OpenAccount` / `CloseAccount` / `PromoteToSummary` / `ImportChart` | Appendix A `Account` fields / `code, effective` / `code, effective` / `template_oid` | schema control |
| `OpenPeriod` … `ReopenPeriod` | `period`, `reason`, (`case` for reopen) | period control |
| `RunClose` | `period`, `mode: DryRun|Execute`, `draft?`, `tasks?: subset` | close run |
| `OpenRestatementCase` … `DiscloseCase` | `kind`, `periods_affected`, `narrative_document?` / `case` / `case` / `case` / `case, disclosure: DocumentRef` | case transitions |
| `AttachEvidence` / `Attest` / `ImportDocument` | `target, documents, role` / `claim, subjects, evidence` / `bytes|locator, kind, mime` | edges / attestation / document |
| `Migrate` | `manifest: {source_system, mode: OpeningOnly | PeriodBalances | FullDetail, trial_balance: DocumentRef, opening_date, chunks: [...]}` inside an open `MigrationRun` | migration batch (§8.6) |
| `IssueStatements` | `definition`, `period`, `certificate: CertificateRef`, `issued_to`, `document?` | `StatementIssuance` (§12.5) |
| `ProposeAdjustment` / `BookAdjustment` / `PassAdjustment` | `engagement`, `entry: PostEntry fields` / `proposal` / `proposal, reason_code` | engagement objects (§11.12) |
| `OpenEngagement` / `CompleteDocumentation` / `Represent` | Appendix A `Engagement` fields / `engagement, day` / `engagement, claims, as_of, document` | engagement control |
| `MarkExhibit` / `Produce` | `ref, label, matter` / `profile, scope, redaction, out` | attestation / bundle with Bates ranges (§13.2) |
| `IssueCapability` / `Revoke` / `LegalHold` / `ReleaseHold` / `ErasureCut` | `holder, caveats, expires` / `commitment, reason` / `scope, authority` / `hold` / `subject, authority` | governance control |
| `ForkDraft` / `PromoteDraft` / `DiscardDraft` / `ForkScenario` / `DiffBranches` | `name` / `draft` / `draft, reason` / `name, from_seq?` / `from: Coordinate, to: Coordinate, scope` | drafts / diff |

Amount parsing: `"1234.56 USD"` is parsed exactly to `{unit: USD, mantissa: 123456}`; `1234.56` as a JSON number is **refused** with the exact string form (a float cannot be trusted to be exact); `"1234.567 USD"` is refused as exceeding the unit's scale with the rounding laws listed as explicit options.

## Appendix C — Entry-Class × Period-State Admissibility Matrix

`Admit` = any principal with `post`; `W(cap[, approvals])` = admitted only with the named capability and approval count; `Refuse` = never. Generated from `admissibility.toml`; the exhaustive test covers every cell.

| Class \ State | `Future` | `Open` | `SoftClosed` | `Closed` | `Locked` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Original` | Refuse | Admit | W(`post_softclosed`) | Refuse | Refuse |
| `Pending` | Refuse | Admit | W(`post_softclosed`) | Refuse | Refuse |
| `Resolution` | Refuse | Admit | Admit | `Posts` W(`post_closed`); `Voids`/`Expires` Admit (the sweep's intrinsic `post_closed`) | `Voids`/`Expires` Admit; `Posts` Refuse |
| `Reversal` | Refuse | Admit | Admit | W(`post_closed`) | Refuse (reverse in next open period instead; the refusal says so) |
| `Replacement` | Refuse | Admit | Admit | W(`post_closed`, 1) | Refuse |
| `Reclassification` | Refuse | Admit | Admit | W(`post_closed`, 1) | Refuse |
| `Adjusting` | Refuse | Admit | Admit | W(`post_closed`, 1) | Refuse |
| `Closing` (incremental, §7.5) | Refuse | W(`close`) | W(`close`) | W(`close`, 1) | W(`restate`, 2) inside an `Approved` case |
| `Allocation` / `Revaluation` / `Elimination` | Refuse | Admit (rule) | Admit (rule) | W(`post_closed`, 1) | Refuse |
| `Migration` | Refuse | Refuse | Refuse | Refuse | W(`migrate`, 2) in `Opening` and `Historical` periods only, inside an open `MigrationRun` (§8.6) |
| `Revision` | Refuse | Refuse | Refuse | W(`restate`, 1) inside a `Revision` case | W(`restate`, 2) inside a `Revision` case |
| `Restatement` | Refuse | Refuse | Refuse | W(`restate`, 2) inside an `Approved` case | W(`restate`, 2) inside an `Approved` case |
| `PriorPeriodAdjustment` (posts in the *current* open period against opening retained earnings; `reason_code.relates_to` names the historical error period) | Refuse | W(`restate`, 2) inside an `Approved` case | W(`restate`, 2) inside an `Approved` case | Refuse | Refuse |
| `PeriodControl` | `Open` only | `SoftClose`, `Close` | `Close`, `Reopen→Open` W(`reopen`, 1) | `Lock` W(`close`, 1), `Reopen` W(`reopen`, 2, case) | `Reopen` W(`reopen`, 2, case) |
| `SchemaControl` / `PolicyControl` | not period-scoped: W(`rule_change`|`schema_change`, 2 for activations) | | | | |

**Period kinds.** A period's kind narrows the matrix before its state decides (`[[kind]]` rows in `admissibility.toml`): a `Historical` period (§7.2) admits only `Migration`, and every other class is refused `FA-REF-010` whatever the period's state; every transition of a `Historical` period is refused `FA-REF-040`. A special period (`Opening`, `Adjustment`, `Closing`) ending on or before the book's opening date is born `Locked`, keeps its kind, admits only `Migration` (others `FA-REF-010`), and refuses every transition (`FA-REF-040`), all by the `[[pre_opening]]` table. Any other `Normal`, `Adjustment`, `Opening`, or `Closing` period is decided by the class × state cells alone. The exhaustive test decides every posting class against every kind row.

Universal rules: an `Original` is never admissible in `Closed` or `Locked`; a `Restatement` is admissible only inside an `Approved` case; the preparer of any entry in a case cannot be one of its approvers; `post_closed` and `restate` caveats may be bounded by `effective ≥ date` so an auditor's adjustment capability cannot reach older periods.

## Appendix D — CLI/MCP Contract

**Envelope (robot mode, one JSON object per line):** `{"v": 1, "event": "<schema-id>", "book": "<id>", "coordinate": {...}, "data": ..., "certificate": "<oid>" | null, "warnings": [...], "next_actions": [{"command": "...", "why": "..."}]}`; every non-zero exit emits exactly one `error` event with `refusal_id`, `exit`, `class`. Diagnostics never appear on stdout. `fa robot-docs schema <event>` prints the JSON schema; fixtures live in `registries/robot_schema/`.

**Exit codes** (strict superset of `fgdb`'s): `0` ok · `2` usage/input · `3` accounting-law or query refusal · `4` environment/open · `5` foundation/I/O failure · `6` conflict · `7` safety block · `8` verification failure. `1` is never emitted. Empty results are `0`.

**Refusal object:** `{"refusal_id": "...", "code": "FA-REF-NNN", "exit": n, "what_failed": "...", "where": {"entry|leg|field|period|account": ...}, "why": {"law": "FA-INV-01.per-unit" | "policy": "sod_policy#restate" | "matrix": {"state": "Closed", "class": "Original"}}, "fix": {"command": "...", "or_field_change": {...}}, "alternatives": [...], "docs": "robot-docs#..."}`.

**Refusal catalog (excerpt; full list in `refusal_catalog.toml`):**

| Code | Name (`refusal_catalog.toml`) | Trigger | `fix` |
|---|---|---|---|
| FA-REF-001 | `UNBALANCED` | Unbalanced per unit | the imbalance per unit; the residual leg suggestion when within one minor unit and a rounding account exists |
| FA-REF-002 | `NOT_A_LEAF` | Leg on a summary account | the leaf children, nearest-by-name first |
| FA-REF-003 | `UNIT_POLICY` | Unit policy mismatch on account | post in the account's unit or choose an account whose policy admits it |
| FA-REF-004 | `UNKNOWN_ACCOUNT` | Unknown account code | `fa chart list --json` to find the account, or `fa chart import` to add it |
| FA-REF-005 | `DIMENSION_MISSING` | Missing required dimension | the dimension and its allowed values (`legal_entity` in multi-entity books) |
| FA-REF-006 | *(reserved)* | Looks like a reversal | `fa reverse <ref> --reason …` |
| FA-REF-007 | `CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED` | `confirm_plan` / `--yes` missing on a dangerous class | the dry-run or safe command to run first, or rerun with `--yes` |
| FA-REF-008 | `SOD` | SoD: approver prepared an entry in the case / self-approval | the policy row; collect approval from a different admissible principal |
| FA-REF-009 | *(reserved alias)* | Idempotency conflict (canonical: `FA-REF-032`) | the recorded digest and outcome; a new key suggestion |
| FA-REF-010 | `PERIOD_LAW` | Effective date in `Closed`/`Locked` (or period state refuses class) | the state, the matrix row, the capability needed, and `fa reclass`/next-period alternative |
| FA-REF-011 | `CAPABILITY` | Capability lacks op/scope | the caveat/operation that blocked; who can issue (`--grant <op>`) |
| FA-REF-012 | `APPROVALS` | Required approvals missing | record the approvals over this proposal digest and resubmit citing them |
| FA-REF-013 | `AGENT_TRUNK` | Agent profile write without draft | `fa draft new …` and the same command with `--draft`, or ask a Human principal |
| FA-REF-014 | `EXPIRED` | Capability expired | obtain a fresh capability; an expired token is never extended |
| FA-REF-015 | `CEILING` | Leg amount exceeds capability ceiling (`amount ≤ A`) | split the work under the ceiling, or have a principal with a higher ceiling post it |
| FA-REF-016 | `APPROVAL_BINDING` | Approval does not bind this proposal digest | re-approve the exact proposal digest being posted |
| FA-REF-017 | `UNKNOWN_APPROVAL` | Unknown approval cited | cite approvals recorded in this book |
| FA-REF-018 | `LEASE_HELD` | Another writer holds the book's writer lease | route via `fad` (automatic) or `fad status` |
| FA-REF-021 | `PERIOD_UNKNOWN` | Unknown period code | `fa period create --fiscal-year <FY>`, then transition the period |
| FA-REF-032 | `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` | Idempotency key reused with different content | use a fresh idempotency key, or resubmit the recorded content exactly |
| FA-REF-034 | `PLAN_INVALIDATED` | Plan invalidated (basis advanced or authority changed) | rehearse the intent again at the current head, review its new certificate, then commit |
| FA-REF-062 | `ALREADY_RESOLVED` | Pending entry already resolved | read the existing resolution with `fa entry show`; a pending entry is resolved once |
| FA-REF-100 | `STRUCTURE` | Structural/syntax refusal (float/over-scale amount, or instant given where a civil date is expected) | exact integer-mantissa string at the unit's scale, or `--effective <YYYY-MM-DD>` |
| FA-REF-127 | `CHECKPOINT_INVALID` | Checkpoint / bundle / proof verification failed | run `fa verify accumulator` and preserve the store for investigation |
| FA-REF-150 | `CERTIFICATE_INVALID` | Report certificate replay or status verification failed | recreate the certificate from the original book and selector |
| FA-REF-154 | `UNIT_UNREGISTERED` | Unregistered unit version in amount | post in a unit registered in `registries/units.toml` (`fa book info --json`) |

**MCP tools:** the clusters of §15.4, each tool documented from `verbs.toml` with Discovery, When-to-use, Parameters, Returns (`next_actions` always), Do/Don't, Examples (JSON-RPC), Common mistakes, Idempotency, Edge cases. Errors are `{error: {type, message, recoverable, data: {fix_hint, suggested_tool_calls, available_options, refusal_id}}}`.

**Determinism:** output ordering canonical; `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` honored; `NO_COLOR`/`CI`/`TERM=dumb`/non-TTY suppress styling and prompts; `verify-determinism`, `verify-stdout-stderr-split`, and `verify-non-tty-discipline` are regression tests.

## Appendix F — Invariant and Evidence Registries

This table contains exactly sixteen top-level invariant IDs. G0 materializes each as one or more stable subordinate clause records in `invariants.toml`; subordinate clause keys do not create additional IDs. Every clause records its exact statement, activation predicate, dependencies, resolvable checker and negative-test entrypoints, model/proof scope, owner, first gate, status, and `waiver = "forbidden"`. CI verifies the sixteen-ID set and table hash, the dependency DAG, checker/test existence, and the activation closure. Statistical, configuration-model, and empirical claims live in their separate registries.

| ID | Invariant | Enforcement |
|---|---|---|
| FA-INV-01 | **Balance law.** For every committed entry and every unit among its legs, debit and credit totals are exactly equal in that unit (`per-unit`); native balancing dimensions enforce equality per `(unit, legal_entity)` (`per-entity`); when base totals are present, base debits equal base credits under the named FX law, separately per legal entity in multi-entity books (`base`), with each representable valuation adjustment recorded and absorbed within its entity (`base-residual`); realized FX uses the paired postings of §5.2 (`realized-fx`); memo and statistical units balance separately (`memo`); every leg posts to a `Leaf` account effective at the entry's date whose unit policy admits the leg's unit (`leaf-only`, `unit-policy`); required dimensions are present (`dimensions`). | Kernel check at step 5 from the payload alone; Canon compiler proof per rule; reference-engine differential; Lean lane for reversal/replacement/reclassification preservation; fuzzed payload decoder. |
| FA-INV-02 | **Append-only.** Every payload object is immutable after commit in every field (`payload-immutable`); every lifecycle object's authoritative history is its sequence of `LifecycleTransition` control entries and its `status` is a rebuildable projection (`lifecycle-by-transition`); correction is a new object with lineage; no path updates or deletes a committed object; schema and registry changes are journaled control entries with empty legs and a typed control payload (`control-record`); unit versions are immutable and historical mantissas are never reinterpreted (`unit-version`). | Substrate immutability (FG-INV-13) plus FrankenAccounting's secure-view facade as the only writer; mutation-attempt negatives; recovery rebuilds derived state only. |
| FA-INV-03 | **Period law.** A period-scoped entry's effective date and special-period discriminator resolve to exactly one permitted period; book-scoped controls follow their registered authority/SoD row without a posting period; the entry's class is admissible for that period's state under the registered matrix and the principal's capability; period transitions occur only through journaled `PeriodControl` entries with the required approvals; `Locked` admits only case-bound restatement classes, case-bound recloses, and reservation releases; the period-state read is a retained point-read witness validated by the substrate's commit-time certifier (first-committer-wins today, SSI when `fgdb-txn` lands) so a transition and a post cannot both commit against stale state; after the latest `Closing` entry of a fiscal year the nominal accounts fold to zero at that known-at (`nominal-zero`). | Generated exhaustive matrix test; conflict oracle over post-vs-close interleavings in both orders under DPOR; reference differential; `fa verify close`; Lean lane for the state machine's reachability. |
| FA-INV-04 | **Registers are folds.** Every native or functional-value register cell, checkpoint, statement row, roll-forward, aging bucket, open-amount cell, lot, and reconciliation state equals the deterministic fold of the visible legs at its coordinate and watermark; a statement definition's mappings partition the leaf accounts it covers (`mapping-partition`); derived structures are never authoritative (inherits FG-INV-18); recomputation from the journal reproduces `fold_digest` bit-for-bit; a mismatch is a correctness defect that discards the derived structure. | `fa verify registers` in CI on every fixture; maintainer crash matrix between delta consumption and watermark publication; reference fold differential; metamorphic suite (rebuild equals maintained). |
| FA-INV-05 | **Evidence closure.** Every committed entry has a registered origin and satisfies the class's registered evidence minimum (`evidence-minimum`); external evidence retains bytes unless an attested by-reference policy downgrades dependent certificates (`external-retained`); supplemental evidence is a new object (`supplemental`); its provenance closure (documents by digest, event, rule version with inputs digest, principal/session, approvals, rate and basis documents, supersession targets) is enumerable and strongly retained for the retention floor; an entry whose origin or evidence references an unknown object is unencodable. | Generated reference checker over Appendix A field rows; retention/GC oracle; `fa explain evidence` conformance; bundle round-trip. |
| FA-INV-06 | **Exactness.** Every amount is an exact fixed-scale integer in a registered unit; every rounding, allocation, FX conversion, and presentation rounding names a registered law; allocations conserve totals exactly; overflow is refused; no float enters any amount path. | Type-level (no float type in `fa-money`); Lean lanes for rounding bounds and allocation conservation; cross-platform determinism harness; property suites. |
| FA-INV-07 | **Bitemporal visibility.** For period-scoped entry selection at `(known_at, effective)`, the creating sequence must lie in the selected known-at prefix and the posting date must satisfy the effective selector, together with the registered branch, lifecycle and migration predicates. Book-scoped controls follow §7.1's known-at control-history and class-applicability rules without a posting date; extract-row selection is distinct from retaining required control/metadata closure; `recorded_at` is only ever the commit stream's assignment; distinct known-at or effective selectors never alias; a known-at view is immutable once its sequence has passed; `AS ORIGINALLY REPORTED` resolves to the first `StatementIssuance` (else the first `Lock`) and `AS REPORTED IN` to a certificate's closure; imported history never double counts with opening balances (`migration-disjoint`). | Reference visibility differential covering Period/Book scopes, control applicability and closure versus extract selection; Lean lane; selector golden corpus; negative fixture for any user-supplied recorded time; migration provenance oracle. |
| FA-INV-08 | **Supersession lineage.** `SUPERSEDES`/`RESOLVES` edges form a DAG over existing targets; every kind has a registered netting law (`net-law`: negation, paired replacement, partial reclassification, or delta); a `Reversal` is the exact side-swap of its target, reverses the target's application effects through an authenticated derived `UNSETTLES` projection (`unsettle`), subject to the exhaustive target-class admissibility table, and an entry is fully reversed at most once by identity (`reversed-once`); a `Replacement` is paired with its reversal in one batch; a pending entry is resolved at most once; a scheduled reversal is cancelled by lineage when its target is superseded; net effects are computable and the originally-posted and currently-stated views agree with the lineage. | Generated reference checker; Lean lane for negation and netting; two-phase resolution model; metamorphic (reverse+replace = replace). |
| FA-INV-09 | **Idempotent intents.** Within one book and principal scope, an idempotency key maps to at most one immutable outcome; retries with identical canonical semantic request bytes return it; differing semantic request bytes are refused; transport-only execution controls are excluded under Appendix B; outcomes are durable before acknowledgement (inherits FG-INV-01). The key-derived immutable `IntentOutcome` is the scoped retry namespace; target-derived Entry identities separately enforce reversal/resolution uniqueness (§4.9). A duplicate outcome is a point conflict, with semantic request equality checked before disclosure. | Retry-storm and lost-response tests under lab; key-conflict negatives; `AlreadyLive`/`IdentitySpent` conformance; outcome-durability crash points. |
| FA-INV-10 | **Authorization before observation; duties segregated.** Capability caveats are enforced before any storage observation (inherits FG-INV-20) or, in the interim posture, by refusal before query construction — never as a result filter; authority is re-evaluated against current policy at commit (`current-policy`), rule-generated effects are authorized after evaluation (`post-evaluation`), ceilings are debited serially (`ceiling`), whole-entry visibility governs scoped reads (`whole-entry`); SoD policy is evaluated at commit for every dangerous class; an approval binds the proposal digest, effects digest, coordinate, and policy epoch of what it approves (`approval-binds-proposal`); a principal cannot approve its own draft's promotion or a case containing its own entries; every external-profile read is logged. | Capability type matrices; SoD negatives for every policy row; approval-digest mismatch negatives; access-log completeness oracle. |
| FA-INV-11 | **Determinism and replay.** Every report, finding, plan, and bundle carries a certificate naming its closure; the same closure reproduces byte-identical canonical output; nondeterministic procedures are seeded and declared; missing or erased closure elements downgrade the completeness grade attributably (inherits FG-INV-19). | Replay CI over every certified artifact; cross-platform digest comparison; erasure-downgrade tests. |
| FA-INV-12 | **Subledger–control reconciliation.** At every committed coordinate the fold of a subledger's detail legs equals its control account's register; external summary feeds must name the detail batch digest they summarize and are refused if the detail does not fold to the summary. | Synchronous kernel check; reference differential; external-feed negatives. |
| FA-INV-13 | **Cross-book and consolidation conservation.** Declared intercompany pairs match under the FX law or carry an explicit difference leg; elimination entries are derived deterministically from matched pairs and the ownership graph; consolidated equity reconciles to the entities' equity plus eliminations and non-controlling interest; book-to-book bridges explain every difference by rule version. | Pair-matching oracle; consolidation replay; bridge completeness test. |
| FA-INV-14 | **Tamper evidence and accumulator continuity.** Each `AccumulatorState` extends the previous (consistency); leaf envelopes bind record kind, coordinate, commit sequence, and within-commit ordinal (`leaf-envelope`); every checkpoint binds a root and a scope-index root the verifier recomputes from the journal prefix; inclusion proofs verify against checkpoints and scope-completeness proofs are sound for their labeled scope (`scope-complete`); public document commitments are recomputable from bytes alone (`public-commitment`); erasure leaves a typed tombstone whose digest the prior proofs still bind; amounts, dates, accounts, and lineage are never in erasure scope. | `fa-verify` as independent oracle; consistency-proof CI across checkpoints; erasure negatives; rollback-detection tests with witnessed checkpoints. |
| FA-INV-15 | **Two-phase and limits.** Pending legs are reserved and the full original pending amounts are removed exactly once at resolution. Posts has one positive balanced sub-vector in Entry.legs with source-ordinal mapping; Voids/Expires has no posting legs and uses the registered ReservationRelease control. Resolution is unique by identity (`sub-vector`, `resolve-once`); reserved availability obeys the §5.4 equations (`availability`); expiry is journaled by sweep under time evidence, never by ambient clock; balance limits are enforced through escrow rights (or, interim, the `LimitCell` reservation of §5.5) and never silently exceeded; `Balancing` legs record the amount actually moved. | Two-phase model under lab with concurrent manual and sweep resolution; escrow conservation inherited from FG-INV-02; limit-breach negatives. |
| FA-INV-16 | **Projections equal payloads.** The derived `Leg` vertices and `HAS_LEG`/`POSTS_TO`/`FOR`/`IN_UNIT` edges of an entry equal its inline legs exactly, are written in the same transaction, and are rebuilt from payloads on recovery; no reader treats a projection as more authoritative than the payload. | Post-commit oracle in sim; recovery rebuild test; projection-drift negatives. |

### Statistical, evidence, and configuration gates (not invariants)

| ID | Qualified claim | Required disclosure/gate |
|---|---|---|
| FA-EVID-01 | Journal-entry-test procedure calibration | population and lookback; per-test parameters; expected flag rate on the clean `fa-gen` scenario; observed rate |
| FA-EVID-02 | Benford conformity | population eligibility rule; digit test; MAD and chi-square with the conformity ranges named; sample size floor |
| FA-EVID-03 | Duplicate and near-duplicate detection | tolerance and similarity thresholds; precision/recall on the labeled corpus |
| FA-EVID-04 | Graph detector performance (layering, kiting, Ponzi motif, shell signature, related-party) | labeled-scenario precision/recall per seed; CGSE policy; complexity witness |
| FA-EVID-05 | Bank-match heuristic confidence | method, features, confidence calibration on confirmed matches; the exact-match rule is not evidence (it is deterministic) |
| FA-CAL-01 | Decision-card threshold tuning | pinned default; tuning principal and engagement; before/after flag rates; fallback |
| FA-CFG-01 | Register watermark-lag capacity model | maintainer throughput model; post rate; predicted versus observed lag; backpressure policy |
| FA-CFG-02 | Expiry-sweep tick capacity | pending population; tick cadence; sweep batch bounds |
| FA-CFG-03 | Retention and durability model | inherits FG-CFG-01 for the book directory; evidence-byte growth model |

## Appendix G — Operation Cost Registry (normative contract)

Each FrankenAccounting operation class maps onto exactly one FrankenGraphDB Appendix G row (its substrate class), and its §17 gate must be derivable from that row's barrier chain. `registries/operation_costs.toml` is the machine-checkable artifact; CI regenerates this projection and byte-compares it. The substrate-class column names the *target protocol* row; today every `write`/`commit` on the embedded handle is one publication (capsule-first, marker-last, two ordered syncs) with no group commit (Appendix H R-GC), and the registry carries a `today` column beside each target row so no gate is derived from a protocol that has not landed.

| Operation class | Substrate class | Substrate transactions | Derived objects minted (FrankenAccounting) | External authorities | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Balance read at watermark | `SnapshotQuery` | 0 | 0 | none | register cell + bounded tail fold |
| Certified report (TB, statement) | `SnapshotQuery` (reads) + 0 writes | 0 | 1 `Certificate` (persisted lazily on request) | none | pure over registers and tail |
| Single-entry post | Autocommit write, fused arm | 1 | 1 `Entry` + *n* `Leg` + edges + `ExternalId`s + 1 `IntentOutcome` | none; a rate lookup is a *document* already in the book, never a synchronous call | group-commit amortized |
| Portable single-entry plan and confirmed commit (R-POST-PLAN) | Full posting preparation, aborted; revalidated preparation and one commit | 0 for planning; 1 for commit, with no additional publication | admitted posting census + 1 immutable graph `Certificate` containing the bounded plan bytes + 1 `CITES` edge | none | same-basis native effects comparison; certificate retention extends the same transaction; no shared mutable object; durable `IntentOutcome` remains a separate unimplemented contract |
| Batch post (*N* entries) | Autocommit write, fused arm (one `WriteBatch`) | 1 | *N* Entry/Leg censuses + one outcome per keyed intent + any separately keyed batch outcome + one retained EffectManifest per approved effect set | none | bounded by `max_batch_entries` |

| Admitted durable refusal | Authorized refusal publication | 1 when published | 1 immutable `IntentOutcome` | none | refusal registry classifies which failures can be durable (§5.9) |
| Release-only resolution | Same bounded resolution publication | 1 | 1 `Resolution` + 0 `Leg` projections + outcome/lineage effects | none | full original pending decrement; no posted increment (§5.4) |

| Effect-manifest closure | Canonical ordered manifest retained before its approvals | 1 additional pre-approval publication if separately persisted; 0 extra if canonically embedded in the existing plan publication | 1 manifest per effect set; O(N) bounded ProposalRefs plus target/authorization references; bytes charged to plan/approval closure | none | never multiply the full manifest per member unless those duplicated bytes are charged; persist/embedding choice is a schema row |
| Draft promotion (*N* planned intents) | Autocommit write, fused arm | 1 | batch census + `DraftSet` transition | none | intent replay with revalidation |
| Period transition | Autocommit write, fused arm | 1 | 1 `PeriodControl` entry | none | SSI witness for racing posts |
| Close run (execute) | sequence of batch posts + transitions | *k* (task count + 1) | per task | none | rehearsal is reads over a draft/scratch book |
| Restatement case post | Batch post | 1 — the case's entries and its `Posted` `LifecycleTransition` are one `write_ordered` transaction, so a crash leaves either nothing or everything | case entries + diff `Certificate` | none | dual-control approvals are prior transactions |
| Register maintenance flush | Autocommit write, fused arm (maintenance class) | 1 per flush tick | touched `Register` cells; periodic `RegisterCheckpoint` | none | off the posting path; amortized |
| Accumulator checkpoint | Autocommit write, fused arm | 1 per cadence | 1 `AccumulatorState` + 1 `TransparencyCheckpoint` | witnesses episodic, never synchronous | peaks derived in commit order |
| Expiry sweep tick | Autocommit write, fused arm | 1 per tick | `Resolution(Expires)` entries | time evidence (substrate's profiled observation) | bounded batch per tick |
| Bundle production | reads | 0 | 1 `Bundle` object (optional persist) | none | deterministic |
| External-profile read (Auditor/Regulator/Investigator/Production) | `SnapshotQuery` + 1 maintenance publication | 1 (the `AccessEvent`, committed before the first protected byte streams; batched per request) | 1 `AccessEvent` | none | internal profiles stay at 0 |
| Ceiling-bearing post | Autocommit write, fused arm | 1 (same transaction) | single-entry census + `UsageCell` CAS | none | the second registered hot-object exception (§14.2) |
| Bulk migration (*M* lines) | substrate bulk protocol (staged chunks, one finalizing transaction) | 1 + chunk staging | `Migration` entries; opening attestation | none | gap-free chunk manifests |

Laws: **derivability** (every §17 gate names its class and counting unit and is consistent with the substrate row's barrier floor); **same-change update** (any change adding a substrate transaction, a synchronous external interaction, or a per-entry derived object updates the registry in the same change); **no hot object on the write path** (a class may not add a shared mutable object — register, accumulator peak, counter — to the posting transaction; the registry's "derived objects" column is checked against the write-path code by the topology gate; the interim `LimitCell` of §5.5 and ceiling-bearing `UsageCell` of §14.2 are the two registered, scoped exceptions, each with its retirement condition recorded).

## Appendix H — Foundation Dependency Ledger (status 2026-10-10)

`registries/foundation_dependencies.toml` copies this table row for row, and fa-registry's tests fail when the two differ or when a `franken-stack.lock` revision drifts from a workspace `Cargo.toml` pin (fa-37f). Current adopted pins and their scopes live in `franken-stack.lock`; each capability row below must be verified against its pinned foundation before use. A foundation's presence does not imply every planned capability is live.

| Row | Foundation capability | Status 2026-10-10 | FrankenAccounting interim posture | Switch-over rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R-RATIONAL | FrankenSymPy `fsym-bigint`, `fsym-rational`, `fsym-budget` at the exact `franken-stack.lock` revision | Integrated in `fa-money`: metered arbitrary-size rates and exact conversion intermediates; native verification required at landing | Fixed-scale `i128` amounts and accounting rounding/FX laws remain owned here; `fgdb-bigint` and the reference engine remain independent oracles | Re-pin only with arithmetic, codec, resource-refusal and dependency-closure gates; no symbolic-engine adoption implied |
| R-API | `fgdb::Database` embedded API: `WriteBatch`, `WriteTxn` (`begin`, point reads, `write_ordered`, `refresh_snapshot`, `commit`), `Database::query`, `execute_certified`, `replay`, `read_session`, `subscribe_native`, `delta_since`/`ensure_delta_window`, `open_memory`, `bulk_load` | Live (single-writer handle) | the legacy `execute_gql*`/`BoundPlan` path is never called | — |
| R-ST | Chronicle system time, `FOR SYSTEM_TIME AS OF SEQ`, retention | Live | — | retention policy per book |
| R-SSI | Full Graph-SSI (`fgdb-txn`) | Planned ("deliberately not SSI" today; pinned WriteTxn already has point/scan witnesses and admitted bounded single-vertex predicate-selection prefixes) | bounded optimistic `WriteTxn` with first-committer-wins over point-read witnesses for dependencies they cover; mutating aggregate-derived plans additionally require §5.10's atomic basis guard. Point-only ordinary posts gain no scan witness or shared hot object. Guard activation requires pinned-foundation conformance and both-order tests; conservative invalidation can require retry or quiescence under the existing soft-close workflow | adopt SSI; the conflict oracle is unchanged |
| R-CAT | Graph catalog (`CREATE GRAPH`) | Absent | label-partitioned regions with `_graph` property | migrate to named graphs with a differential run |
| R-VT | `ValidTimeContract`, slices, `FOR VALID_TIME`, temporal non-overlap | Planned (normative) | `effective_date` and effectivity bounds as indexed properties; `fa-time` interval algebra in the kernel; pinned point/scan witnesses where they cover the dependency, with §5.10's atomic basis guard for aggregate-derived mutations; no index-range witnesses | shadow-build/validate/activate with the full §16 temporal suite on both representations |
| R-ESC | Escrow domains (balance limits without hot locks) | Planned | per-limited-account `LimitCell` reservation cells updated by CAS (§5.5, §0.4 decision 7) | registered decision row; abort-rate gate reported in both |
| R-REG | Ripple materialized views | Planned | `fa-registers` maintainer actor over `delta_since` (pull-only; `ensure_delta_window` on open; rebuild from the last `RegisterCheckpoint` on `DeltaCursorRetired`; woken by `fad` after each commit) with checkpoints and tail folds | replace the maintainer body; readers unchanged |
| R-SUB | `subscribe_native` / `SUBSCRIBE TO` | Live, in-process only, no durable resume, requires an unrestricted-scope capability | used for incremental findings and live triage post-1.0 once scoped capabilities and durable resume land | — |
| R-BR | Branch fork/merge, `DIFF` family | Read selectors live; fork/merge planned | drafts as intent logs (promotion = replay = merge-ladder rung 1); scenarios as scratch-book replay via `open_memory`; a `Migration` chunk carries no `settles` (legacy applications inside a `MigrationRun` are refused `SETTLE_SHAPE`; each legacy open item is imported net of its legacy applications, §8.6) | drafts gain optional branch materialization; scenarios become forks; promotion stays intent replay; legacy applications inside a `FullDetail` run return on a staged branch (or fa-omi's staged applications) whose open amounts are proven equal to the fold |
| R-IDX | Beacon durable property/range indexes; FTS/HNSW | In-memory property *equality* index per published snapshot only; no durable, range, or commit-maintained index; no range witnesses; FTS/HNSW per-call | accelerators only, never semantics; the posting transaction never scans (§7.8) | — |
| R-PRISM | `CALL fnx.*` over `SnapshotGraphView` | Live subset | direct `fnx` over Lantern projections where the subset lacks an algorithm | — |
| R-WARDEN | Macaroon caveats compiled to planner predicates; redaction; audit pipeline | Types live; compilation in progress; audit pipeline planned | caveats evaluated by Steward before query construction (refusal, never result filtering); FrankenAccounting access log; redaction by document-class storage separation | adopt compiled caveats and substrate redaction; FA-INV-10 clause text updated by registered decision |
| R-CERT | `execute_certified` → `NativeResultCertificate`, `replay`, `ReplayManifest` grades | Live for native reads including `fnx` procedure evidence; not for writes or subscriptions | FrankenAccounting `Certificate.inputs` embeds every `NativeResultCertificate` and replay calls `Database::replay` per input (§13.6) | — |
| R-POST-PLAN | `WriteTxn::staged_effect_digest` and application certificate object retention | Native staged effects digest is live; the embedded API exposes no generic application object storage or portable write-witness export | rehearse through the posting kernel and abort; authenticate the portable plan with a book-scoped key; require the same basis and repeat all laws and native effects at commit; atomically retain a graph `Certificate` and entry `CITES` edge. Public digests are locators; `Entry.plan_certificate` stays absent. Existing approvals are required before planning | adopt native certificate object references only when public staging and retaining-reference APIs exist; adopt witness-based rebasing only with a verified portable witness closure; preserve already retained graph certificates |
| R-MMR | §5.8 accumulator, transparency checkpoints, witness policies, verifier SDK | Planned | journal-level accumulator and checkpoints (permanent design) | cross-anchor `AccumulatorState` into the substrate MMR; two independent chains |
| R-SIGN | Ed25519 (RFC 8032) key derivation and signing in `fgdb-crypto` | Absent | in-house signer in `fa-notary` (`sign`): constant-time by construction (fixed-base table, mask-select behind an optimization barrier, no secret-dependent branch or index), timed per host by a dudect harness (statistical evidence per run, fa-cs80); `fa-verify` keeps its own independent verifier | when `fgdb-crypto` ships Ed25519 at a pinned revision, sign through it and remove the in-house signer (requested: frankengraphdb#3) |
| R-VM | `fgdb-udf-vm` deterministic bytecode | Planned | Canon IR interpreter under stripped `Cx`; IR frozen | compile IR to VM; differential on every golden corpus |

| R-EXP | `ExpiryContract` / epoch advance | Planned | FrankenAccounting expiry sweep control entries under time evidence | adopt the contract for pending-entry visibility; sweep remains for resolutions |
| R-CLI | `fgdb` robot-mode conventions (`--robot` first, `event`-discriminated NDJSON, `robot schema`, exit codes 0/2/3/4/5) | Live (`doctor`/`--json` are the substrate's W10 target) | matched by `fa`; `fa`'s dictionary is a strict superset | — |
| R-SRV | `fgdbd` server posture, FGP | Live (bounded) | not used in 1.0; `fad` is embedded + local RPC + MCP | post-1.0 networked posture |
| R-LAB | `fgdb-sim`, lab VFS, crash matrices | Live for bounded scenarios | `fa-sim` extends | — |
| R-HLC | Wall-clock/HLC instant on `CommitMarker` | Planned (`commit_hlc` carries the sequence number) | journaled `TimeEvidence` ticks (§7.1a); `AS KNOWN AT <instant>` resolves through ticks and certificates record the bracketing ticks | substrate HLC becomes primary; ticks remain as corroboration |
| R-CON | Key/existence/cardinality/check constraints (`fgdb-constraints`, `fgdb-secure-view`) | Planned | identity-derived `vid`s (§4.9) make duplicates point conflicts; required dimensions and leaf-only are kernel checks | constraints become a redundant guard, never a replacement |
| R-SYM | Durable label/property/relation catalog | Absent (host-interned ids; host supplies `GraphSymbolResolver`) | `registries/symbols.toml` frozen at G0, journaled at book creation, checked by `fa doctor` | adopt the substrate catalog with a differential run |
